Refined applications of Kato’s Euler systems for modular forms Thanks: Competing interests: The author declares none.
Chan-Ho Kim

Abstract
. We discuss refined applications of Kato’s Euler systems for modular forms of higher weight at good primes (with more emphasis on the non-ordinary ones) beyond the one-sided divisibility of the main conjecture and the finiteness of Selmer groups. These include a proof of the Mazur–Tate conjecture on Fitting ideals of Selmer groups over p p -cyclotomic extensions and a new interpretation of the Iwasawa main conjecture via the non-triviality of Kato’s Kolyvagin systems with structural applications. Some applications to Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer conjecture are also discussed.



2010 Mathematics Subject Classification
11F67, 11G40, 11R23

1.
Introduction

In this article, we study the more refined nature of Kato’s Euler systems and its applications to the Iwasawa theory for modular forms of higher weight at good primes over the cyclotomic tower.
In particular, we focus on
the Mazur–Tate conjecture on Fitting ideals of Selmer groups
[
MT87
]
(Theorem
1.3
) and the Iwasawa main conjecture à la Kato
1
1

1

We follow Kato’s formulation for the
main conjecture
in this article if there is no further specification.

[
Kat04
]
and Conjecture A à la Coates–Sujatha
[
CS05
]
(Theorem
1.5
).
Before stating our main results, we need to introduce some notation.

Let
p
be an odd prime and fix embeddings
\iota_{p}:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and
\iota_{\infty}:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
.
Let
f=\sum_{n\geq 1}a_{n}(f)q^{n}\in S_{k}(N,\psi)
be a newform with character
\psi
and assume that
(N,p)=1
.
Let
F_{\pi}=\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\iota_{p}(a_{n}(f));n\geq 1)
,
\mathcal{O}_{\pi}
the ring of integers of
F_{\pi}
,
\pi
a uniformizer (which is compatible with
\iota_{p}
) lying above
p
, and
\mathbb{F}_{\pi}=\mathcal{O}_{\pi}/\pi\mathcal{O}_{\pi}
the residue field of
F_{\pi}
.
Write
\overline{f}=\sum_{n\geq 1}a_{n}(\overline{f})q^{n}\in S_{k}(N,\overline{\psi})
where
a_{n}(\overline{f})
and
\overline{\psi}
are the complex conjugates of
a_{n}(f)
and
\psi
, respectively.
For any field
L
, denote by
G_{L}
the absolute Galois group of
L
.

Let

\rho_{f}:G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathrm{Aut}_{F_{\pi}}(V_{f})\simeq\mathrm{GL}_{2}(F_{\pi})

be the
\pi
-adic continuous cohomological Galois representation associated to
f

[
Kat04
,
\lx@sectionsign
14.10]
.
We say that
\rho_{f}
has large image
if the image of
G_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})}
under
\rho_{f}
contains a conjugate of
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
,
We fix a Galois-stable
\mathcal{O}_{\pi}
-lattice
T_{f}\subseteq V_{f}
and
the residual representation is denoted by
\overline{\rho}=\overline{\rho}_{f}
.
Denote by
N(\overline{\rho})
the prime-to-
p
Artin conductor of
\overline{\rho}
and by
W_{f}:=V_{f}/T_{f}
the discrete Galois module associated to
f
.
Let
\omega
be the Teichmüller character and write
T_{f,i}:=T_{f}\otimes\omega^{i}
and
W_{\overline{f},-i}=W_{\overline{f}}\otimes\omega^{-i}
.
Write
M(r):=M\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}(r)
to be the
r
-th Tate twist of a Galois module
M
for
r\in\mathbb{Z}
. From now on, we assume that
1\leq r\leq k-1
.

1.1.
Mazur–Tate conjecture

In
[
MT87
]
, Mazur and Tate studied “Iwasawa theory for elliptic curves over
finite
abelian extensions over
\mathbb{Q}
” and formulated the “weak” main conjecture for elliptic curves (Conjecture
1.2
).
The
finite layer
Iwasawa theory due to Mazur and Tate is more complicated than the usual Iwasawa theory (over the
infinite
extension) since neither we can directly apply the theory of Iwasawa modules to finite layer objects nor we can ignore “finite errors”.
In addition, the structure of the Iwasawa algebra is much simpler than that of the group rings for the finite layer objects.
This finite layer Iwasawa theory with the use of Fitting ideals is a part of
refined Iwasawa theory

[
Kur03a
,
Kur12
,
Kur14b
]
.

In order to consider the most interesting case, we put the following condition in this subsection.

Assumption 1.1
.

k
is even and
r=k/2
, i.e. we focus on the central critical
L
-values.

Since it is known that the non-central critical
L
-values of modular forms do not vanish, the central critical one is only interesting from the point of view of the “weak” vanishing conjecture
[
MT87
, Conj. 1]
, which is an equivariant analogue of the rank part of Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer conjecture.

The following conjecture is known as the “weak” main conjecture
[
MT87
, Conj. 3]
2
2

2

The original conjecture concerns general abelian extensions of
\mathbb{Q}
.
, which basically claims Mazur–Tate elements are contained in the Fitting ideals of dual Selmer groups of modular forms over finite extensions.

Conjecture 1.2

(Mazur–Tate)
.

Assume
k
is even and
\overline{\rho}
is irreducible.
Then

\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}}),k/2}(\overline{f})\in\mathrm{Fitt}_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}})/\mathbb{Q})]}\left(\mathrm{Sel}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}}),W_{\overline{f}}(k/2))^{\vee}\right)

where

\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}}),k/2}(\overline{f})
is the Mazur–Tate element of
\overline{f}
“at
s=k/2
” over
\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}})
normalized by the optimal periods (reviewed in
\lx@sectionsign
3.2
), and
(-)^{\vee}
is the Pontryagin dual.

It is known that the “weak” vanishing conjecture is an easy corollary of the “weak” main conjecture (Corollary
8.3
).
When an elliptic curve has good ordinary reduction at
p
, Conjecture
1.2
follows from the standard ingredients of Iwasawa theory
[
KK21
,
\lx@sectionsign
2]
. Notably, the control theorem applies well in the good ordinary setting.
In
[
KK21
]
, the author and Kurihara gave a simple proof of the Mazur–Tate conjecture for elliptic curves with supersingular reduction with
a_{p}(E)=0
by using the
\pm
-Iwasawa theory of elliptic curves. See also
[
Kat21
]
.
In fact, the first significant progress towards the Mazur–Tate conjecture for modular forms (at non-ordinary primes) was made in the work of Emerton–Pollack–Weston
[
EPW
]
, which was first announced around 2010, and their argument relies heavily on the
p
-adic local Langlands correspondence à la Colmez.

Recall that the
fine Selmer group of
W_{\overline{f}}(k/2)
over
\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}})

\mathrm{Sel}_{0}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}}),W_{\overline{f}}(k/2))\subseteq\mathrm{Sel}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}}),W_{\overline{f}}(k/2))

is defined by putting the strict local condition at
p
.
The
simplified
version of the first main theorem of this article is as follows.

Theorem 1.3

(Theorem
8.1
)
.

Let
p
be an odd prime and
f\in S_{k}(N,\psi)
a newform such that
(N,p)=1
and
\rho_{f}
has large image.
We assume that

(FL)

2\leq k\leq p-1
and
k
is even,

(
\alpha\neq\beta
)

X^{2}-a_{p}(f)X+\psi(p)p^{k-1}
has distinct roots,

(non-ord)

\overline{\rho}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
is irreducible, and

(fin)

\mathrm{Sel}_{0}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}),W_{\overline{f}}(k/2))^{\vee}
has no non-trivial finite Iwasawa submodule.

Then, for
n\geq 1
, Conjecture
1.2
holds

(1.1)

\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}}),k/2}(\overline{f})\right)\subseteq\mathrm{Fitt}_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}})/\mathbb{Q})]}\left(\mathrm{Sel}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}}),W_{\overline{f}}(k/2))^{\vee}\right).

Our approach is completely different from that of our former work.
No signed Iwasawa theory is applicable since the notion of finite layer signed objects are unclear yet when
a_{p}(f)\neq 0
.
It is known that there are many examples to satisfy the condition (fin) on fine Selmer groups
[
KK21
, Ex. 1.21]
.

We first study the failure of control theorem of Selmer groups in the non-ordinary case.
We explicitly analyze the error term in the control theorem and relate the Fitting ideals of the error terms with Perrin-Riou’s
\theta
-elements, the finite layer analogue of Perrin-Riou’s algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions.
Perrin-Riou’s
\theta
-elements and Mazur–Tate elements are also related by Kato’s Euler system divisibility and the Euler system construction of Mazur–Tate elements.

The Euler system construction of Mazur–Tate elements comes from the construction of the
integral
finite layer Coleman maps, which send
integral
Kato’s Euler systems to
integral
Mazur–Tate elements.
As an expert can expect, our approach is based on an integral refinement of Perrin-Riou’s local Iwasawa theory via integral
p
-adic Hodge theory and Wach modules.

However, a delicate integrality question naturally arises from the generalization and is not precisely detected before.
How to make
three different
integral properties arising from finite layer Coleman maps, Kato’s Euler systems, and Mazur–Tate elements compatible (Conjecture
1.10
)?
We partially resolve this problem (Theorem
1.11
).
For example, the construction of the integral finite layer Coleman maps given in
[
EPW
]
is not strong enough to deduce (
1.1
) since a certain constant error term is inevitable in their work (Remark
1.17
).
Our construction removes their constant error term when
2\leq k\leq p-1
and precisely controls the error term even for modular forms of arbitrary weight (Theorem
8.1
and Remark
8.2
).
This shows the novelty of our local Iwasawa theory which is extensively discussed in
\lx@sectionsign
1.3
.

1.2.
Kurihara numbers and the main conjecture

Let
E/\mathbb{Q}
be an elliptic curve.
In a series of his papers
[
Kur03a
,
Kur12
,
Kur14b
,
Kur14a
]
, Kurihara introduced numerical invariants
\widetilde{\delta}_{m}(E)\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
attached to
E
, which we call
Kurihara numbers
, in the context of
Kolyvagin systems of Gauss sum type
and proved a number of remarkable theorems relating these invariants to the size and structure of the Selmer group of
E
over
\mathbb{Q}
. This is another part of
refined Iwasawa theory
.

In
[
KKS20
]
, the author and his collaborators recognized the direct connection between Kato’s Euler systems and Kurihara numbers.
We established a simple numerical criterion for the verification of the main conjecture for weight two modular forms at good primes via Kurihara numbers.
The argument of
[
KKS20
]
relied heavily on working with weight two modular forms because the formal groups of modular abelian varieties plays a crucial role in the computation of the integral image of the dual exponential map.
In this present work, we extend the work of
[
KKS20
]
to higher weight modular forms by instead using the integral finite layer Coleman maps mentioned earlier.
The construction of the integral finite layer Coleman maps completely replaces the computation of the dual exponential map.
By using the rigidity of
\Lambda
-adic Kolyvagin systems, we also show the converse to the main result of
[
KKS20
]
, so the numerical criterion itself is equivalent to the Iwasawa main conjecture with no Tamagawa defect
3
3

3

“No Tamagawa defect” means the
\pi
-indivisibility of Fontaine–Perrin-Riou’s local Tamagawa numbers
[
FPR94
]
.
. See also
[
Sak22
]
for a different approach.
This equivalence yields two applications.
First, we can use the numerical criterion to verify the Iwasawa main conjecture.
Conversely, by establishing the Iwasawa main conjecture, we obtain the non-triviality of Kato’s Kolyvagin systems and the non-vanishing of Kurihara numbers, so we are able to describe the structures of
p
-strict Selmer groups and usual Selmer groups in terms of Kato’s Kolyvagin systems and (a higher
p
-power version of) Kurihara numbers, respectively.
In particular, the latter one provides a completely new structural application of the Iwasawa main conjecture. See
[
MR04
,
Kimb
,
Kima
]
for details.

Unfortunately, the mod
p
numerical criterion
must fail
when the Tamagawa defect is non-trivial.
In order to handle this issue, we use congruences of modular forms.
In the ordinary case, we can spread knowledge of the main conjecture of Mazur–Greenberg from one form to all congruent forms if we know the vanishing of
\mu
-invariant of the
p
-adic
L
-function of one form and the one-sided divisibility of the main conjecture
[
GV00
,
EPW06
]
.
The same type of result for Kato’s main conjecture is proved in
[
KLP
]
under the vanishing of
\mu
-invariant of Kato’s zeta elements.
We also provide another numerical criterion to verify the
\mu=0
for Kato’s zeta elements.
Since modular forms of minimal level (in the sense of
[
Ser87
]
) have no Tamagawa defect
[
KO23
]
, these two numerical criteria are enough to verify the main conjecture for families of modular forms.

We say that a prime
\ell
is a
Kolyvagin prime
if
\ell\nmid Np
,
\ell\equiv 1\pmod{p}
, and
a_{\ell}(f)\equiv\psi(\ell)+1\pmod{\pi}
.
Denote by
\mathcal{N}
the set of square-free products of Kolyvagin primes.
For each Kolyvagin prime
\ell
, we fix a primitive root
\eta_{\ell}
and define the corresponding discrete logarithm

\mathrm{log}_{\eta_{\ell}}(a)\in\mathbb{Z}/(\ell-1)\mathbb{Z}
by
\eta^{\mathrm{log}_{\eta_{\ell}}(a)}_{\ell}\equiv a\ (\text{mod}\ \ell)
.
Denote by
\overline{\mathrm{log}_{\eta_{\ell}}(a)}\in\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
the mod
p
reduction of
\mathrm{log}_{\eta_{\ell}}(a)
.
For
1\leq r\leq k-1
,
a\in\mathbb{Q}
, and
m\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
,
\lambda^{\pm,\mathrm{opt}}(\overline{f},z^{r-1};a,m)
is the optimally normalized modular symbol defined in
\lx@sectionsign
3
and
\overline{\lambda^{\pm,\mathrm{opt}}(\overline{f},z^{r-1};a,m)}
its mod
\pi
reduction.

Definition 1.4
.

Let
m\in\mathcal{N}
.

(1)

When
i=0
, we define the
(mod
\pi
) Kurihara numbers for
W_{\overline{f}}(r)
at
m
by

\widetilde{\delta}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(\overline{f},r):={\displaystyle\sum_{a\in(\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\left(\prod_{\ell|m}\overline{\mathrm{log}_{\eta_{\ell}}(a)}\right)\cdot\overline{\lambda^{\pm,\mathrm{opt}}(\overline{f},z^{r-1};a,m)}}\in\mathbb{F}_{\pi}.

where the sign of modular symbols coincides with that of
(-1)^{r-1}
.

(2)

When
i\neq 0
, we also define the
(mod
\pi
) Kurihara numbers for
W_{\overline{f},-i}(r)
at
m
by

\widetilde{\delta}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(\overline{f},i,r):={\displaystyle\sum_{a\in(\mathbb{Z}/mp\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\left(\prod_{\ell|m}\overline{\mathrm{log}_{\eta_{\ell}}(a_{(m)})}\right)\cdot\overline{\omega^{i}(a_{(p)})}\cdot\overline{\lambda^{\pm,\mathrm{opt}}(\overline{f},z^{r-1};a,mp)}}\in\mathbb{F}_{\pi}

where

a_{(m)}\in(\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
is the mod
m
reduction of
a
,

a_{(p)}\in(\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
is the mod
p
reduction of
a
, and the sign of modular symbols coincides with that of
(-1)^{r-1}\cdot\omega^{i}(-1)
.
Here,
\overline{\omega^{i}(a_{(p)})}
means the mod
p
reduction of
\omega^{i}(a_{(p)})
.

It is known that the non-vanishing question of Kurihara numbers is well-defined although the values depend on various choices.

The
collection of Kurihara numbers for
W_{\overline{f}}(r)
or
W_{\overline{f},-i}(r)
is defined by

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\delta}}(\overline{f},r)=\left\{\widetilde{\delta}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(\overline{f},r)\in\mathbb{F}_{\pi}:m\in\mathcal{N}\right\}\textrm{ or }\ \widetilde{\boldsymbol{\delta}}(\overline{f},i,r)=\left\{\widetilde{\delta}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(\overline{f},i,r)\in\mathbb{F}_{\pi}:m\in\mathcal{N}\right\},

respectively. We say that either one does not vanish if

(1.2)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\widetilde{\delta}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(\overline{f},r)\neq 0&\textrm{ when }i=0,\\
\widetilde{\delta}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(\overline{f},i,r)\neq 0&\textrm{ when }i\neq 0\end{array}\right.

for some square-free product
m
of Kolyvagin primes.

We are ready to state the
simplified
form of the second main result of this article, which summarizes
Theorem
9.1
, Theorem
10.1
, and Corollary
1.12
.

Theorem 1.5
.

Let
p\geq 5
be a prime and
f\in S_{k}(N,\psi)
a newform such that
(N,p)=1
and
\rho_{f}
has large image.
Let
r
be an integer with
1\leq r\leq k-1
such that
r\neq\frac{k-1}{2}
(e.g.
k
is even).
We assume that

(FL)

2\leq k\leq p-1
,

(
\alpha\neq\beta
)

X^{2}-a_{p}(f)X+\psi(p)p^{k-1}
has distinct roots, and

(“non-CM”)

\overline{\rho}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
is not a direct sum of two characters.

Then the following three statements are equivalent.

(1)

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\delta}}(\overline{f},r)
or
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\delta}}(\overline{f},i,r)
does not vanish as in (
1.2
).

(2)

Kato’s Kolyvagin system for
T_{f,i}(k-r)
is primitive.

(3)

The main conjecture holds for
T_{f,i}(k-r)
(Conjecture
4.15
) and the local Tamagawa numbers are not divisible by
\pi
.

In this case, the module structure of the
p
-strict Selmer group
\mathrm{Sel}_{0}(\mathbb{Q},W_{\overline{f},-i}(r))
is determined by Kato’s Kolyvagin system for
T_{f,i}(k-r)
.

Corollary 1.6
.

Keep all the assumptions in Theorem
1.5
.

(1)

If

(1.3)

\sum_{a\in(\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\overline{\lambda^{\pm,\mathrm{opt}}(\overline{f},z^{r-1};a(1+bp),p^{n})}\cdot\overline{\omega^{i}(a)}\neq 0

where the sign of modular symbols coincide with that of
(-1)^{r-1}\cdot\omega^{i}(-1)

for some
b\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{n-1}\mathbb{Z}
with
n\geq 1
, then the
\mu
-part of the main conjecture and Conjecture A of Coates–Sujatha (Conjecture
4.17
) for
T_{f,i}(k-r)
hold.

(2)

If both mod
p
numerical criteria (
1.2
) and (
1.3
) hold for
T_{f,i}(k-r)
,
then
the main conjecture and
Conjecture A of Coates–Sujatha
for
T_{f_{0},i}(k-r)
hold
where
f_{0}
runs over all newforms of weight
k
and level
N_{f_{0}}
such that
p\nmid N_{f_{0}}
and
\overline{\rho}_{f_{0}}\simeq\overline{\rho}
.

Remark 1.7
.

When
f
is
p
-ordinary, Corollary
1.6
.(2) extends to all members in the Hida family of
\overline{\rho}

[
EPW06
]
.
Indeed, the
r=\frac{k-1}{2}
case can be also treated by using Tate twists
[
Rub00
,
\lx@sectionsign
6.5]
.
It is expected that (
\alpha\neq\beta
) always holds and the large image assumption implies (“non-CM”). See
\lx@sectionsign
1.4
for the detail.

Theorem
1.5
establishes the equivalence among three different statements.
Since the non-triviality of Kolyvagin systems has the application to the structures of
p
-strict Selmer groups
[
MR04
]
, Theorem
1.5
provides a new application of the Iwasawa main conjecture to the structures of Selmer groups.
The application to the structure of Bloch–Kato Selmer groups is studied in subsequent works
[
Kimb
,
Kima
]
in a more general setting.
Theorem
1.5
can also be interpreted as the equivalence between the strong version of “Kolyvagin’s conjecture for Kato’s Kolyvagin systems”
[
Kol91
,
Zha14
]
and the Iwasawa main conjecture with no Tamagawa defect.
Kurihara first formulated the numerical criterion (
1.2
) as a conjecture for elliptic curves with good ordinary reduction
[
Kur14b
, Conj. 1, page 320]
.

Corollary
1.6
reduces the main conjecture and Conjecture A of Coates–Sujatha
for families of modular forms
to
two easily verifiable and effectively computable
4
4

4

An efficient algorithm for the numerical verification of the Iwasawa main conjecture for elliptic curves is implemented at
https://github.com/aghitza/kurihara_numbers
due to Alexandru Ghitza
[
Kim21
, Appendix A]
. Extensive numerical examples for the main conjecture are computed in
[
KKS20
,
\lx@sectionsign
8]
.
An algorithm for the Iwasawa invariants for elliptic curves with good reduction is implemented at
https://github.com/rpollack9974/Iwasawa-invariants
due to Robert Pollack and is used in
http://www.lmfdb.org
.

mod
p
numerical criteria (
1.2
) and (
1.3
)
for one modular form
by upgrading the one-sided divisibility of the main conjecture.
Theorem
1.5
covers ordinary and non-ordinary cases on equal footing.
In other words, all the applications of the main conjecture and Conjecture A of Coates–Sujatha become numerically verifiable.
These include Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer conjecture (§
11.1
and
11.2
), Bloch–Kato’s Tamagawa number conjecture for modular forms (Corollary
9.3
), and other main conjectures
5
5

5

e.g. the (equivalent) main conjecture of Mazur–Greenberg
[
Kat04
,
\lx@sectionsign
17]
, the (equivalent) signed main conjectures
[
LLZ10
]
, and the Heegner point main conjecture for elliptic curves
[
Zha14
,
BCK21
]
.
.

1.3.
Mazur–Tate elements and the “optimal” choice of Kato’s zeta elements

The common key feature of the proofs of
Theorem
1.3

and Theorem
1.5

is the construction of the integral finite layer Coleman maps, which turn Kato’s Euler systems into Mazur–Tate elements over
general
cyclotomic extensions of
\mathbb{Q}
.
Let
\alpha
and
\beta
be the roots of
X^{2}-a_{p}(f)X+\psi(p)p^{k-1}
with
\mathrm{ord}_{p}(\alpha)\leq\mathrm{ord}_{p}(\beta)

where
\mathrm{ord}_{p}
is normalized by
\mathrm{ord}_{p}(p)=1
.
It is known than the complex absolute values of
\alpha
and
\beta
(under any complex embedding) are
p^{\frac{k-1}{2}}

[
Kat04
, (14.10.5)]
.

Assumption 1.8
.

As we have seen, the following conditions are assumed throughout this article.

(LI)

\rho_{f}
has large image.

(
\alpha\neq\beta
)

When
f
is non-ordinary at
p
, we assume
\alpha\neq\beta
.

(“non-CM”)

When
f
is ordinary at
p
, we assume that
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
is not a direct sum of two characters.

(ENV)

When
r=\frac{k-1}{2}
is an integer,
\overline{\alpha}\neq\zeta\cdot p^{\frac{k-1}{2}}
where
\zeta
is a root of unity.

It is expected that (
\alpha\neq\beta
) always holds and (non-split) also holds under (LI) (Remark
1.16
).

Let
m
be a positive integer with
(m,Np)=1
.
In
\lx@sectionsign
4
,
for
\gamma\in T_{f}
, we construct the
canonical
Kato’s Euler system

z_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}}),\gamma}(f,k-r)

and its variation

\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}}),\gamma}(f,k-r)

in
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}}),T_{f}(k-r))

under the large image assumption.
If
\gamma\in V_{f}
, then these cohomology classes lie in

\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}}),V_{f}(k-r))
in general.
The choice of
\gamma
corresponds to the choice of the periods in Kato’s explicit reciprocity law (Theorem
4.3
).
Denote by

\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}}),r}(\overline{f})\in\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})/\mathbb{Q})]

the Mazur–Tate element of
\overline{f}
“at
s=r
” over
\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})
normalized by the optimal periods (reviewed in
\lx@sectionsign
3
).

The technical heart of this article is the following Euler system construction of Mazur–Tate elements.

Theorem 1.9

(Mazur–Tate elements)
.

Keep all the statements in Assumption
1.8
.
Then there exist the finite layer Coleman map over
\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})

\mathrm{Col}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}}),r}:\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(k-r))\to F_{\pi}[\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})/\mathbb{Q})]

and the crystalline normalization of the optimal periods via Kato’s period integral

\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}\in V_{f}

such that

\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}}),r}(\overline{f})=\mathrm{Col}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}}),r}\left(\mathrm{loc}_{p}\left(\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}}),\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,k-r)\right)\right)\in\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})/\mathbb{Q})]

where
r
is an integer with
1\leq r\leq k-1
.

Furthermore, the restriction of
\mathrm{Col}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}}),r}
to
T_{f}
becomes integral, i.e. we have

(1.4)

\mathrm{Col}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}}),r}:\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(k-r))\to\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})/\mathbb{Q})].

Proof.

See
\lx@sectionsign
7
for the proof.
Especially,
the recipe of
\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}
is given in
\lx@sectionsign
7.3
,
and
the construction of
\mathrm{Col}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}}),r}
is given in
\lx@sectionsign
7.8
.
See also
\lx@sectionsign
4
for the construction of
\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}}),\gamma}(f,k-r)
for
\gamma\in V_{f}
.
∎

Theorem
1.9
is the finite layer, integral, and equivariant refinement of the local Iwasawa theory à la Perrin-Riou
[
PR94
,
Ben00
]
.
A substantial amount of this article is devoted to the construction of the finite layer Coleman maps and the study of their integrality property.
Although we follow the well-known philosophy
6
6

6

“The
p
-adic
L
-functions of modular forms can be obtained by putting Kato’s Euler systems into Perrin-Riou’s local machinery.”
of Perrin-Riou’s local Iwasawa theory,
the technical details become extremely delicate if we aim to obtain this level of generality and precision.
As mentioned before, the main difficulty arises from the comparison among three different integrality properties:

(1)

The uniform integrality of Mazur–Tate elements over general cyclotomic extensions depending only on the
optimal
choice of complex periods
\Omega^{\pm}_{\overline{f}}
. This is the content of
\lx@sectionsign
3
.

(2)

The uniform integrality of the canonical Kato’s Euler systems over general cyclotomic extensions depending only on the choice of
\gamma\in V_{f}
. This is the content of
\lx@sectionsign
4
(e.g. Definition
4.10
).

(3)

The uniform integrality of the finite layer Coleman maps over general cyclotomic extensions depending only on the choice of
\eta\in\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{\overline{f}})
. This is the content of
\lx@sectionsign
7
(e.g. Proposition
7.9
).

The constructions of the (non-signed and infinite layer) Coleman maps for non-ordinary higher weight modular forms usually do not care the integrality and cover the
p
-cyclotomic extensions only
[
PR94
,
Kat04
,
LLZ10
,
Lei11
]
.
On the other hand, the constructions based on the formal groups of modular abelian varieties
[
Kur02
,
Kob03
,
Ots09
,
Ota18
,
KKS20
,
Kat21
]
can control the integrality of the finite layer Coleman maps (e.g.
[
Kur02
,
\lx@sectionsign
3]
) but these methods do not generalize to higher weight modular forms.
This integrality comparison issue is also noticed in the work of Emerton–Pollack–Weston
[
EPW
]
, but they just leave the difference as a constant error term. See Remark
1.17
.

We deal with this integrality issue as follows.
We first choose the complex periods
\Omega^{\pm}_{\overline{f}}
by the optimal periods in modular symbols (“integral” complex Hodge theory)
since the optimal integrality of Mazur–Tate elements is essential for the arithmetic applications.
All the complex periods are controlled
up to
p
-adic units
.
Then we choose a suitable
\varphi
-stable lattice in the Dieudonné module associated to
V_{\overline{f}}
via Wach modules (integral
p
-adic Hodge theory) to have the
integral
finite layer Coleman maps.
As a result, the notion of the crystalline normalization
\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}\in V_{f}
of the optimal periods naturally appears in the Euler system side.
In other words, the choice of
\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}\in V_{f}
makes the complex periods in the Mazur–Tate element
optimal
and the finite layer Coleman map
integral

simultaneously
.
The cost is that we do not know whether
\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}\in T_{f}
in general; in other words, the integrality of the canonical Kato’s Euler systems associated to the optimal periods is
unclear
.
This obscurity leads us to formulate Conjecture
1.10
below, and we prove it in many cases (Theorem
1.11
).

We now need to introduce more notation for the precise description.
Let

\Gamma=\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})/\mathbb{Q})

and
\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}w}=\mathcal{O}_{\pi}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
be the Iwasawa algebra.
Decompose

\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}w}=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{p-2}\Lambda^{(i)}_{\mathrm{Iw}}

where
\Lambda^{(i)}_{\mathrm{Iw}}:=\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}w}/(\sigma-\omega^{i}(\sigma):\sigma\in\Gamma)
.
Let

j:\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}}))\to\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{p^{n}},1/p])

be the natural map.
For
a\in\{1,2\}
, the
a
-th Iwasawa cohomology for
T_{f}(k-r)
over
\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}w}
is defined by

\mathrm{H}^{a}_{\mathcal{I}w}(T_{f}(k-r)):=\varprojlim_{n}\mathrm{H}^{a}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{p^{n}},1/p]),j_{*}T_{f}(k-r))

and
\mathrm{H}^{a}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f,i}(k-r))
is defined by the
\omega^{i}
-isotypic component of
\mathrm{H}^{a}_{\mathcal{I}w}(T_{f}(k-r))

where
i\in\{0,1,\cdots,p-3,p-2\}
.
We use
\mathrm{H}^{a}_{\mathcal{I}w}
for the extension
\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})/\mathbb{Q}
and
\mathrm{H}^{a}_{\mathrm{Iw}}
for the cyclotomic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-extension
\mathbb{Q}_{\infty}
of
\mathbb{Q}
.

Let

\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{prim}}(T_{f}(k-r)):=\left\langle\mathbf{z}_{\mathbb{Q},\gamma}(f,k-r):\gamma\in T_{f}\right\rangle\subseteq\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(T_{f}(k-r))

be the module generated by primitive Kato’s zeta elements recalled in
\lx@sectionsign
4
.
Denote by
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{prim}}(T_{f,i}(k-r))\subseteq\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f,i}(k-r))
the
\omega^{i}
-isotypic component of
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{prim}}(T_{f}(k-r))
.

Let
T^{\pm}_{f}
be the
\mathcal{O}_{\pi}
-submodule of
T_{f}
whose eigenvalue is
\pm 1
with respect to the complex conjugation via (
3.1
) below, and
\gamma^{\pm}_{0}
a
\mathcal{O}_{\pi}
-generator of
T^{\pm}_{f}
, respectively.
Write
\gamma_{0}=\gamma^{+}_{0}+\gamma^{-}_{0}
.
Then it is well-known that

\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{prim}}(T_{f}(k-r))=\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}w}\mathbf{z}_{\mathbb{Q},\gamma_{0}}(f,k-r)

and
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{prim}}(T_{f,i}(k-r))=\Lambda^{(i)}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\mathbf{z}_{\mathbb{Q},\gamma_{0}}(f,i,k-r)
(Proposition
4.11
).

When
p
does not divide
N
, we also have the zeta element
\mathbf{z}_{\mathbb{Q},\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,k-r)
associated to
\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}\in V_{f}

described in Theorem
1.9
and denote by

\mathbf{z}_{\mathbb{Q},\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,i,k-r)
the image of
\mathbf{z}_{\mathbb{Q},\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,k-r)
in
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f,i}(k-r))
.
Since we do not know whether
\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}\in T_{f}
,
it is even unclear for us whether

\mathbf{z}_{\mathbb{Q},\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,k-r)\in\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{prim}}(T_{f}(k-r))
.
However, since
\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}
is determined by optimal periods, we
believe
that
\mathbf{z}_{\mathbb{Q},\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,k-r)
is the “most correct” zeta element for the formulation and the verification of the Iwasawa main conjecture, at least when
p\nmid N
.
Since Kato’s main conjecture is formulated with
\mathbf{z}_{\mathbb{Q},\gamma_{0}}(f,k-r)
, it is natural to ask the following question.

Conjecture 1.10
.

Assume that
\rho_{f}
has large image (Assumption
1.8
).
Let
T_{f}
be a Galois-stable
\mathcal{O}_{\pi}
-lattice of
V_{f}
and
r\in\mathbb{Z}
.
If
p
does not divide
N
, then

\Lambda^{(i)}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\mathbf{z}_{\mathbb{Q},\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,i,k-r)=\Lambda^{(i)}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\mathbf{z}_{\mathbb{Q},\gamma_{0}}(f,i,k-r)

in
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f,i}(k-r))
for all
i=0,\cdots,p-2
.

Conjecture
1.10
claims that two different integral normalizations of Kato’s zeta elements coming from modular symbols and Galois representations should coincide up to units; especially,
\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}\in T_{f}
.
Conjecture
1.10
allows us to make the precise connection between Theorem
1.9
and the main applications (Theorem
1.3
and Theorem
1.5
).

We say that

\overline{\rho}
is
p
-ordinary
if the restriction of
\overline{\rho}
to
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
is reducible,
and
\overline{\rho}
is
p
-distinguished
if the semi-simplification of the restriction of
\overline{\rho}
to
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
is not a scalar.

Theorem 1.11
.

Suppose that
\overline{\rho}
is irreducible.
If

(1)

(N,p)=1
and
2\leq k\leq p-1
or

(2)

\overline{\rho}
is
p
-ordinary and
p
-distinguished,

then
\delta^{+}_{f,\mathrm{cris}}
and
\delta^{-}_{f,\mathrm{cris}}
generate
T_{f}
over
\mathcal{O}_{\pi}
. In particular, Conjecture
1.10
follows under Condition (1) or (2). In this case, the canonical periods are optimal periods.

Proof.

See
\lx@sectionsign
7.9
.
∎

Theorem
1.11
shows that all three integral properties mentioned above are compatible
when
2\leq k\leq p-1
or the residual representation is
p
-ordinary and
p
-distinguished.
Indeed, the Fontaine–Laffaille range assumption in
Theorem
1.3
and Theorem
1.5

can be replaced by Conjecture
1.10
(with some modification for the latter).
See Theorem
8.1
, Theorem
9.1
, and Theorem
10.1
for their general versions.
See also (
7.14
) for the picture of the integrality comparison.

1.4.
Further remarks

We rephrase Corollary
1.6
.(2) from the viewpoint of the residual representation and discuss its generalization.

Corollary 1.12
.

Let
p\geq 5
be a prime and
2\leq k\leq p-1
an integer.
Let

\overline{\rho}:\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})

be a continuous, odd, and irreducible Galois representation such that

•

the image of
\overline{\rho}
contains a conjugate of
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
;

•

the restriction of
\overline{\rho}
to
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
does not admit the trivial subrepresentation.

Let
S_{k}(\overline{\rho})
be the set of newforms of weight
k
defined by the following criterion:

a newform
f
of level
N_{f}
lies in
S_{k}(\overline{\rho})
if and only if
p\nmid N_{f}
and
\overline{\rho}\simeq\overline{\rho}_{f}
.

If there exists a newform
f_{0}\in S_{k}(\overline{\rho})
such that

(1)

N_{f_{0}}=N(\overline{\rho})
,

(2)

\alpha_{0}\neq\beta_{0}
in the sense of Assumption
1.8
,

(3)

f_{0}
and
r
satisfy Assumption
1.8
.(ENV),

(4)

the numerical criterion in Theorem
1.5
.(1) holds, and

(5)

the numerical criterion in Corollary
1.6
.(1) holds,

then the main conjecture and Conjecture A of Coates–Sujatha hold for
T_{f,i}(k-r)
where
f
runs over all forms in
S_{k}(\overline{\rho})
.

Proof.

It follows from the combination of Theorem
1.5
, Corollary
1.6
, and
[
KLP
]
.
∎

Remark 1.13
.

Combining Corollary
1.12
with the recent work
7
7

7

There are also some related work by Colmez–Wang
[
CWb
]
, Fouquet–Wan
[
FW
]
, and Yiwen Zhou
[
Zho
]
.
of Nakamura
[
Nak23
]
, which extends
[
KLP
]
to the universal deformation ring, we obtain the following statement.
We keep the following assumptions.

•

p\geq 5
,

•

the image of
\overline{\rho}
contains a conjugate of
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
,

•

\mathrm{End}_{\mathbb{F}_{\pi}[G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}]}(\overline{\rho}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}})=\mathbb{F}_{\pi}
, and

•

\left(\overline{\rho}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\right)^{ss}\not\simeq\left(\mathbf{1}\oplus\omega^{\pm 1}\right)\otimes\chi

for any character
\chi:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\to\mathbb{F}^{\times}_{\pi}
.

Let
S(\overline{\rho})
be the set of newforms of arbitrary weight and arbitrary level whose residual representations are isomorphic to
\overline{\rho}
.
If there exists a newform
f_{0}\in S(\overline{\rho})
satisfying all the conditions in Corollary
1.12
,
then
the main conjecture and Conjecture A of Coates–Sujatha hold for
T_{f,i}(k-r)
where
f
runs over all forms in
S(\overline{\rho})
.
It is remarkable that this result is insensitive to the range of weight and the type of primes.
In other words,

the Iwasawa main conjecture and the
\mu=0
conjecture for modular forms of arbitrary weight and level at an arbitrary prime could be verified from two numerical computations on one specific form (or possibly two forms)
.
Nakamura’s work depends heavily on Emerton’s local-global compatibility
[
Eme
]
.

Remark 1.14
.

In order to prove various types of main conjectures, there are two approaches in general: the Eisenstein congruence method and the Euler system method
[
Kat07
,
\lx@sectionsign
2.4 and
\lx@sectionsign
2.5]
.
Kato himself proved one-sided divisibility of his main conjecture via the Euler system method
[
Kat04
]
(Theorem
4.16
).
Recent years, there has been significant progresses towards the opposite divisibility via the Eisenstein congruence method.
In
[
SU14
]
, Skinner–Urban prove the opposite divisibility of the main conjecture for
T_{f,i}(k-r)
when

f
is good ordinary at
p
,
\psi=\mathbf{1}
,
k\equiv 2\pmod{p-1}
,
r=k-1
,
i=0
, and there exists a prime
\ell
exactly dividing the tame level such that
\overline{\rho}
is ramified at
\ell
.
Building on this work, the multiplicative case is also carried out in
[
Ski16
]
.
In
[
Wan15
]
, the condition on the level is replaced by the existence of a real quadratic field satisfying a certain integral property.
In all the literature on the Eisenstein congruence approaches, the conditions
\psi=\mathbf{1}
and
i=0
are imposed as well as the critical point
r
is fixed. In addition, the ordinary and non-ordinary cases are studied separately and there are certain tame level assumptions therein.
On the other hand, Theorem
1.5
does
not
require
\psi=\mathbf{1}
and does cover all critical points and the
full
cyclotomic extension
\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})
by varying
r
and
i
. We also do
not
impose any condition on the tame level and deal with ordinary and non-ordinary cases on equal footing.

Remark 1.15
.

If the Tamagawa defect is non-trivial, then all Kurihara numbers must vanish
[
MR04
, Prop. 6.2.6]
.
The minimal level condition
N=N(\overline{\rho})
ensures that there is no Tamagawa defect
[
EPW06
, Lem. 4.1.2]
.
See also
[
Büy11
, (H.T), Page 3150]
.
A congruent form of minimal level can be always found in the set of congruent newforms via level lowering, at least in the Fontaine–Laffaille range when
p\geq 5

[
DT94
]
. See
[
KO23
]
for a more quantitative analysis of this aspect.

Remark 1.16
.

We discuss the motivation of Assumption
1.8
.

(LI)

The large image assumption prevents CM forms.
It is satisfied for all Galois-stable lattices if it is satisfied for one lattice for non-CM forms
[
Kat04
, Rem. 12.8]
.
Thus, the choice of
T_{f}
does not affect any of result of this article. This condition is important in the construction of integral canonical Kato’s Euler systems.

(
\alpha\neq\beta
)

This assumption is used to “
p
-destabilize” the local points à la Perrin-Riou constructed in
[
PR94
]
in order to construct Mazur–Tate elements of modular forms.
This holds when
k=2
and is expected to hold in general because it follows from the Tate conjecture on algebraic cycles
[
CE98
]
.

(“non-CM”)

This condition is equivalent to the non-existence of mod
p
companion forms
[
Gro90
,
CV92
]
, and it is also equivalent that
\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},W_{\overline{f},-i}(r))=0
for
i=0,\cdots,p-2

[
Gro90
, Prop. 13.2]
8
8

8

The unchecked compatibility in
[
Gro90
]
is confirmed in
[
Cai07
]
.
.
Since this means that
f
is not congruent to an anomalous ordinary CM form, this must always hold under (LI).
See
[
PS13
,
LZ13
,
CWE22
]
for this topic.

(ENV)

This condition naturally appears in the computation of local Iwasawa theory (e.g. Proposition
7.9
) and the root of unity
\zeta
comes from the equivariant refinement. This condition also implies the non-vanishing of the
p
-th Euler factor of
\overline{f}
at
r
, and it is automatic when
k
is even.

Remark 1.17
.

We discuss some remarks on Theorem
1.9
.

(1)

In
[
EPW
]
, a completely different construction of the finite layer Coleman maps over
\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}})
is given by using the
p
-adic local Langlands correspondence for
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
(e.g.
[
Col10b
,
Col10a
]
) for de Rham representations when
\rho_{f}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
is irreducible. It does not require the (
\alpha\neq\beta
) assumption.
However, the integrality of the finite layer Coleman map is
less
controlled therein; as a result, the constant error term
C_{f}
in Theorem
8.1
is not specified in the main theorem of
[
EPW
]
.

(2)

In
[
Col98
]
and
[
Nak14
]
, Colmez and Nakamura generalized Perrin-Riou’s local Iwasawa theory to de Rham representations and de Rham
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules, respectively, but it seems that the construction of the local points is missing in their work.

(3)

Theorem
1.9
shows that the Euler system and modular symbol constructions of Mazur–Tate elements
precisely
coincide. On the way, we also confirm that the Kato–Perrin-Riou
p
-adic
L
-function
precisely
coincides with the Mazur–Tate–Teitelbaum
p
-adic
L
-function at good primes (Theorem
7.19
). See
[
Ota23
]
for a similar construction.
As far as we know, this level of precision was not known before at least for higher weight non-ordinary modular forms.

Remark 1.18
.

It seems reasonable to expect that the Euler system construction of Mazur–Tate elements over general cyclotomic extensions has a variety of arithmetic applications as that of
p
-adic
L
-functions has. We expect this idea would apply to other Euler systems (e.g. see
[
LZ
,
LZ20
]
for Loeffler–Zerbes’ program on the construction of new Euler systems for higher rank groups).

1.5.
Structure of this article

The first half (from
\lx@sectionsign
2
to
\lx@sectionsign
6
) of this article can be regarded as a preliminary part except the construction of canonical Kato’s Euler systems in
\lx@sectionsign
4
. In the second half (from
\lx@sectionsign
7
to
\lx@sectionsign
10
), we prove the main theorems in this article.

In
\lx@sectionsign
2
, we recall the convention and the basic facts on Galois representations arising from modular forms, Galois cohomology and Selmer groups, and Iwasawa cohomology.
In
\lx@sectionsign
3
, the relevant comparison isomorphisms among the realizations of modular motives and Kato’s period maps are reviewed. Then we discuss the modular symbol construction of Mazur–Tate elements following
[
MTT86
]
and pin down the optimal periods depending only on the values of modular symbols. Then we compare Kato’s period integrals with the optimal periods.
In
\lx@sectionsign
4
, we review two kinds of Kato’s Euler systems and construct the canonical Kato’s Euler systems.
We also prove that the canonical Kato’s Euler system is integral under the large image assumption and recall the statements of the main conjecture and Conjecture A of Coates–Sujatha.
In
\lx@sectionsign
5
, we briefly review
p
-adic Hodge theory with emphasis on its integral aspect via Wach modules. Especially, we discuss an integral
\varphi
-stable lattice of the crystalline Dieudonné module constructed by Benois.
In
\lx@sectionsign
6
, we study Perrin-Riou’s local Iwasawa theory and its integral refinement following Benois’ approach.

In
\lx@sectionsign
7
, we construct
p
-stabilized equivariant local points and equivariant
p
-adic
L
-functions of
p
-stabilized modular forms from Kato’s Euler systems via the Perrin-Riou’s local machinery.
Especially, we confirm these Kato–Perrin-Riou
p
-adic
L
-functions and the
p
-adic
L
-functions of Mazur–Tate–Teitelbaum constructed from modular symbols are the same ones.
Then we “
p
-destabilize”
p
-stabilized equivariant local points and construct the finite layer Coleman maps (Theorem
1.9
).
In
\lx@sectionsign
8
, we prove the “weak” main conjecture of Mazur–Tate (Theorem
8.1
). We review how the control theorem fails when
f
is non-ordinary at
p
and recall some properties of Fitting ideals. After doing some local computation, we give a step-by-step proof of Theorem
8.1
.
In
\lx@sectionsign
9
, we prove the mod
p
numerical criterion for Kato’s main conjecture (Theorem
9.1
) by the explicit computation of mod
p
Kolyvagin derivatives of Mazur–Tate elements.
In
\lx@sectionsign
10
, we prove the mod
p
numerical criterion for Conjecture A of Coates–Sujatha (Theorem
10.1
) and discuss its application to elliptic curves (Corollary
10.2
) by the explicit computation of modular symbols and Mazur–Tate elements.

In
\lx@sectionsign
11
, we discuss arithmetic applications of our main results to Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer (BSD) conjecture.
There have been significant progresses in BSD conjecture in recent years.
The
p
-converse to a theorem of Gross–Zagier and Kolyvagin and the
p
-part of the BSD formula for rank one elliptic curves are enormously developed
[
Kob13
,
Kob14
,
Zha14
,
Ven16
,
BBV16
,
JSW17
,
Cas18
,
Wan20
,
Ski20
,
BT20
,
Wan
,
Spr
,
BPS
,
CÇSS
,
CWa
,
Kim23
]
.
We discuss the applications of these mod
p
numerical criteria to the
p
-part of the BSD formula
for elliptic curves of analytic rank one at a good supersingular prime
p
following Kobayashi’s
p
-adic Gross–Zagier formula
[
Kob13
]
.
We also study the applications of these mod
p
numerical criteria to the Heegner point context based on the work of Wei Zhang on Kolyvagin’s conjecture
[
Zha14
]
.
More precisely, we are able to numerically verify Kolyvagin’s conjecture for any good prime
p>3
.

In other words, we offer the numerical verification method of all the results of Wei Zhang for modular abelian varieties at any (non-ordinary) good prime
p>3
.

2.
Galois and Iwasawa cohomologies

2.1.
Construction of Galois representations

We follow
[
Kat04
, (4.5.1) and
\lx@sectionsign
8.3]
.
Let
N\geq 4
and
\varpi:E\to Y_{1}(N)
the universal elliptic curve over the modular curve and

\mathcal{H}^{1}_{p}:=\mathrm{R}^{1}\varpi_{*}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
the étale
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-sheaf on
Y_{1}(N)
.
We define

V_{k,\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y_{1}(N)):=\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(Y_{1}(N)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathrm{Sym}^{k-2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{p}))
,

V_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}(f):=V_{k,\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y_{1}(N))\otimes_{\mathbb{T}}\mathbb{T}/\wp_{f}
, and

V_{F_{\pi}}(f):=V_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}(f)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}

where
\mathbb{T}
is the Hecke algebra over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
faithfully acting on
V_{k,\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y_{1}(N))
and
\wp_{f}
is the height one prime ideal of
\mathbb{T}
generated by the Hecke eigensystem of
f

following
[
Kat04
,
\lx@sectionsign
6.3]
.
Here,
V_{F_{\pi}}(f)
is our
V_{f}
.
More explicitly,
\rho_{f}
satisfies the following properties:

(1)

\mathrm{det}(\rho_{f})=\chi^{1-k}_{\mathrm{cyc}}\cdot\psi^{-1}

where
\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}
is the
p
-adic cyclotomic character;

(2)

for any prime
\ell
not dividing
Np
, we have

\mathrm{det}\left(1-\rho_{f}(\mathrm{Frob}^{-1}_{\ell})\cdot u:\mathrm{H}^{0}(I_{\ell},V_{f})\right)=1-a_{\ell}(f)\cdot u+\psi(\ell)\cdot\ell^{k-1}\cdot u^{2}

where
\mathrm{Frob}_{\ell}
is the arithmetic Frobenius at
\ell
in
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}}/I_{\ell}
and
I_{\ell}
is the inertia subgroup of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}}
;

(3)

for the prime number
p
lying under
\pi
, we have

\mathrm{det}\left(1-\varphi\cdot u:\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f})\right)=1-a_{p}(f)\cdot u+\psi(p)\cdot p^{k-1}\cdot u^{2}

where
\varphi
is the Frobenius operator acting on
\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f}):=(V_{f}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbf{B}_{\mathrm{cris}})^{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}

[
Fon94
]
.
Here,
\mathbf{B}_{\mathrm{cris}}
is the crystalline period ring recalled in
\lx@sectionsign
5
.

When
N<4
, we are still able to construct Galois representations using the
N\geq 4
case and trace maps.

2.2.
Galois cohomology and Selmer groups

Let
\ell
be any rational prime and
K_{v}
a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
.
Let
M=T_{f}
,
V_{f}
, or
W_{f}
or their Tate twists.
We write

\mathrm{H}^{1}(K_{v},M):=\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}_{v}/K_{v}),M)
.
Also, for a number field
K
and a rational prime
\ell
, we define the semi-local cohomology groups by

\mathrm{H}^{1}(K\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},M):=\bigoplus_{v|\ell}\mathrm{H}^{1}(K_{v},M)

where
v
runs over the primes of
K
dividing
\ell
.
The localization map at
p
is denoted by

\mathrm{loc}_{p}:\mathrm{H}^{1}(K,M)\to\mathrm{H}^{1}(K\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},M)
.
For a place
v
of
K
dividing
p
, we define

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f}(K_{v},V_{f}(j))

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{ker}\left(\mathrm{H}^{1}(K_{v},V_{f}(j))\to\mathrm{H}^{1}(K_{v},V_{f}(j)\otimes\mathbf{B}_{\mathrm{cris}})\right),

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f}(K_{v},W_{f}(j))

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{im}\left(\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f}(K_{v},V_{f}(j))\to\mathrm{H}^{1}(K_{v},W_{f}(j))\right),\textrm{ and}

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f}(K_{v},T_{f}(j))

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{preim}\left(\mathrm{H}^{1}(K_{v},T_{f}(j))\to\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f}(K_{v},V_{f}(j))\right).

For a place
v
of
K
not dividing
p
, we define

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f}(K_{v},V_{f}(j))

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{ur}}(K_{v},V_{f}(j)),

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f}(K_{v},W_{f}(j))

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{im}\left(\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f}(K_{v},V_{f}(j))\to\mathrm{H}^{1}(K_{v},W_{f}(j))\right),\textrm{ and}

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f}(K_{v},T_{f}(j))

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{preim}\left(\mathrm{H}^{1}(K_{v},T_{f}(j))\to\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f}(K_{v},V_{f}(j))\right).

Since we assume
p>2
, we ignore the infinite place. See
[
Rub00
, Rem. 1.3.7]
.
For notational convenience, we write
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{/f}(-)=\dfrac{\mathrm{H}^{1}(-)}{\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f}(-)}
.
The natural finite-singular quotient of the localization map at
p
is denoted by

\mathrm{loc}^{s}_{p}:\mathrm{H}^{1}(K,M)\to\mathrm{H}^{1}(K\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},M)\to\mathrm{H}^{1}_{/f}(K\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},M)
.

Let
\Sigma
be a finite set of places of
\mathbb{Q}
containing
p
,
\infty
, and the bad primes for
\overline{f}
.
For a number field
K
,
K_{\Sigma}
is the maximal extension of
K
unramified outside the places lying over the places in
\Sigma
. Let
r
be an integer.
We define the (discrete)
Selmer group of
W_{\overline{f}}(r)
over
K
by

\mathrm{Sel}(K,W_{\overline{f}}(r)):=\mathrm{ker}\left(\mathrm{H}^{1}(K_{\Sigma}/K,W_{\overline{f}}(r))\to\prod_{\ell\in\Sigma}\dfrac{\mathrm{H}^{1}(K\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},W_{\overline{f}}(r))}{\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f}(K\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},W_{\overline{f}}(r))}\right)

where
\mathrm{H}^{i}(F/K,M)=\mathrm{H}^{i}(\mathrm{Gal}(F/K),M)
.

Let
\mathbb{Q}_{\infty}\subseteq\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})
be the cyclotomic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-extension of
\mathbb{Q}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{n}
the subextension of
\mathbb{Q}
in
\mathbb{Q}_{\infty}
of degree
p^{n}
.
We define the Selmer group over the cyclotomic tower
\mathbb{Q}_{\infty}
or
\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})
by taking the direct limit.

2.3.
Structure of global and local Iwasawa cohomology

We recall the comparison of Galois and étale cohomologies over the cyclotomic tower with a slight equivariant generalization.

Let
m
be an integer
\geq 1
with
(m,p)=1
, and
F
a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}
in
\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{\infty}})
.
Let
j_{F}:\mathrm{Spec}(F)\to\mathrm{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{F}[1/p])
be the natural map and
write

\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),T_{f}(k-r)):=\varprojlim_{F}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{F}[1/p]),j_{F,*}T_{f}(k-r))

where
F
runs over finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}
in
\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{\infty}})
.

Proposition 2.1

(Kurihara)
.

Let
m\geq 1
be an integer and
\Sigma_{m}=\Sigma\cup\{q,\textrm{ a prime}:q|m\}
.
Then we have an isomorphism

\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),T_{f}(k-r))\simeq\varprojlim_{F}\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\Sigma_{m}}/F,T_{f}(k-r))

where
F
runs over finite extensions of
\mathbb{Q}
in
\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{\infty}})
.

Proof.

It follows from the localization exact sequence of étale cohomology.
See
[
Kur02
,
\lx@sectionsign
6]
,
[
Kob03
, Prop. 7.1.i)]
, and
[
KLP
, Prop. 5.1]
.
Although the
m=1
case is treated in the reference, the proof goes through verbatim due to
[
Kat04
, Lem. 8.5]
.
∎

As a consequence of Proposition
2.1
,
\varprojlim_{F}\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\Sigma_{m}}/F,T_{f}(k-r))
is independent of
\Sigma
.
We also have the decomposition

\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),T_{f}(k-r))=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{p-2}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),T_{f,i}(k-r))
.
Here,

\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),T_{f,i}(k-r)):=\varprojlim_{F}\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\Sigma_{m}}/F,T_{f,i}(k-r))

where
F
runs over finite extensions of
\mathbb{Q}
in
\mathbb{Q}_{\infty}(\zeta_{m})
.

Lemma 2.2
.

If
\rho_{f}
has large image, then
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),T_{f}(k-r))
is free over
\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}w}
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the statement for any integer
r
. Put
r=k
.
If the image of
\overline{\rho}
contains a conjugate of
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
, we first claim

(2.1)

\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\overline{\rho})=0.

(1)

Suppose that
\#\mathbb{F}_{\pi}>4
.
Let
\overline{\rho}:\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\pi})
and
H:=\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})/\mathrm{ker}(\overline{\rho})
. Thus, we have
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{p})\subseteq H\subseteq\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\pi})
by assumption. Since
\mathrm{PSL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\pi})
is simple if
\#|\mathbb{F}_{\pi}|>4
, the maximal abelian quotient of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\pi})
is
\mathbb{F}^{\times}_{\pi}
via the determinant. Thus, for any abelian extension
F
of
\mathbb{Q}
, the image of
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/F)
under
\overline{\rho}
contains
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
. There is no fixed point of
(\mathbb{F}_{\pi})^{\oplus 2}
under
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
-action.

(2)

Suppose that
\mathbb{F}_{\pi}=\mathbb{F}_{3}
. Since
\overline{\rho}
is odd,
\overline{\rho}
is surjective.
The image of
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}))
under the map

\textstyle{\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\rho}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{3})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{pr}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{3})\simeq S_{4}}

is isomorphic to
S_{4}
,
A_{4}
,
V_{4}
(the Klein four group), or the trivial group.
Because

(\mathrm{pr}\circ\overline{\rho})(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q}))/(\mathrm{pr}\circ\overline{\rho})(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})))

is abelian,
S_{4}
and
A_{4}
are only possibilities and
\mathrm{PSL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{3})\simeq A_{4}
. Since
A_{4}
is a unique subgroup of
S_{4}
of index 2, the image of
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}))
contains
\mathrm{PSL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{3})
in
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{3})
. Due to
[
Ser89
, Lem. 2, IV-23]
, the image of
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}))
contains
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{3})
in
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{3})
.
Thus, there is no fixed point of
(\mathbb{F}_{3})^{\oplus 2}
under the action of
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}))
via
\overline{\rho}
.

Due to (
2.1
),

\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),T_{f})\otimes_{\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}w}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}w}/(\gamma-1)

is a free
\mathcal{O}_{\pi}
-module
where
\gamma
is a generator of
\Gamma=\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})/\mathbb{Q})
.
Then the map defined by multiplication by
\gamma-1

\times(\gamma-1):\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),T_{f})\to\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),T_{f})

is injective.
Thus,
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),T_{f})
is free over
\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}w}
. See also
[
Sak18
, Rem. 6.5]
.
∎

3.
Mazur–Tate elements I: Betti construction

We review the modular symbol construction of Mazur–Tate elements for modular forms.
This construction works for modular forms of arbitrary weight and level at any odd prime.
We closely follow
[
MTT86
]
.

3.1.
Realizations of modular motives

We recall several realizations of motives from modular forms and their identifications in
[
Kat04
]
.
Let
N\geq 4
for this moment.

We first recall the Betti cohomology of modular curves in
[
Kat04
,
\lx@sectionsign
4.5,
\lx@sectionsign
5.4, and
\lx@sectionsign
6.3]
.
Let
\varpi:E^{\mathrm{univ}}\to Y(M,N)
be the universal elliptic curve and we define a local system
\mathcal{H}^{1}
on
Y(M,N)(\mathbb{C})
by

\mathcal{H}^{1}=\mathrm{R}^{1}\varpi_{*}(\mathbb{Z})
,
which is locally isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
.
See
[
Kat04
,
\lx@sectionsign
2.1]
for the definition of
Y(M,N)
.
For any commutative ring
A
, we define

\displaystyle V_{k,A}(Y(M,N))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{H}^{1}(Y(M,N)(\mathbb{C}),\mathrm{Sym}^{k-2}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathcal{H}^{1})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}A),

\displaystyle V_{k,A}(Y_{1}(N))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{H}^{1}(Y_{1}(N)(\mathbb{C}),\mathrm{Sym}^{k-2}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathcal{H}^{1})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}A)

by using the natural quotient map
Y(M,N)
to
Y_{1}(N)
for the latter.

We recall the étale cohomology of modular curves in
[
Kat04
,
\lx@sectionsign
8.3]
.
We define a smooth étale sheaf
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{p}
on
Y(M,N)_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
by
\mathrm{R}^{1}\varpi_{*}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.
Then
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{p}\simeq\mathcal{H}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}
as sheaves on
Y(M,N)(\mathbb{C})
.
For
A=\mathbb{Z}_{p}
,
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, or
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
(
n\geq 1
), we consider

\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(Y(M,N)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathrm{Sym}^{k-2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}A),}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(Y_{1}(N)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathrm{Sym}^{k-2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}A)}

where the latter is defined by using the natural quotient map as before.

We identify Betti cohomology with étale cohomology

(3.1)

V_{k,A}(Y_{1}(N))\simeq\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(Y_{1}(N)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathrm{Sym}^{k-2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}A)

for
A=\mathbb{Z}_{p}
,
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, or
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
(
n\geq 1
).
Via (
3.1
),
V_{k,A}(Y_{1}(N))
admits a continuous action of
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
for such
A
’s.
One can construct the above objects even for
N<4
by using the
N\geq 4
case and the trace map as in
\lx@sectionsign
2.1
.

Now we focus on one cuspidal eigenform via the projection
[
Kat04
,
\lx@sectionsign
6.3]
.
Let

F=\mathbb{Q}(a_{n}(f);n\geq 1)\subseteq\mathbb{C}
,
which is a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}
and is stable under the complex conjugation.

Let
M_{k}(\Gamma_{1}(N))
be the space of modular forms of weight
k
and level
\Gamma_{1}(N)
as a
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space.
We define the
F
-submodule
\wp^{\mathrm{dR}}_{f}
of
M_{k}(\Gamma_{1}(N))\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}F
generated by the images of
T_{n}\otimes 1-1\otimes a_{n}(f)
for all
n\geq 1
where
T_{n}
is the
n
-th Hecke operator.
Let
S(f):=\left(M_{k}(\Gamma_{1}(N))\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}F\right)/\wp^{\mathrm{dR}}_{f}
which is a one-dimensional
F
-vector space.

We define the
F
-submodule
\wp^{\mathrm{B}}_{f}
of
V_{k,F}(Y_{1}(N))
generated by the images of
T_{n}\otimes 1-1\otimes a_{n}(f)
for all
n\geq 1
.
Let
V_{F}(f)=V_{k,F}(Y_{1}(N))/\wp^{\mathrm{B}}_{f}
which is a two dimensional
F
-vector space.

For any
F
-algebra
A
, we define

V_{A}(f):=V_{F}(f)\otimes_{F}A

and

V_{F_{\pi}}(f)
admits a continuous Galois action as in
\lx@sectionsign
2.1
via (
3.1
), i.e. the notation is compatible with that in
\lx@sectionsign
2.1
.

For any
x\in V_{F}(f)
,
x^{\pm}:=\frac{1}{2}(1\pm\iota)x
where
\iota
is induced from the complex conjugation.
Let
V_{F}(f)^{\pm}:=V_{F}(f)^{\iota=\pm 1}
and then
\mathrm{dim}_{F}\ V_{F}(f)^{\pm}=1
, respectively.

Following
\lx@sectionsign
3.1
, the realizations of modular motives have the following comparisons

(3.2)

\textstyle{V_{F_{\pi}}(f)}
\textstyle{V_{F}(f)\otimes F_{\pi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{V_{F}(f)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V_{F}(f)\otimes\mathbb{C}}
\textstyle{S(f)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{per}_{f}}

and
\mathrm{per}_{f}
is Kato’s period map reviewed in
\lx@sectionsign
4.3
.

3.2.
Modular symbols, Mazur–Tate elements, and optimal periods

We follow the convention of
[
MTT86
,
\lx@sectionsign
1,
\lx@sectionsign
3, and
\lx@sectionsign
4]
.

Let
S_{k}
be the space of all cuspforms of weight
k
and level
\Gamma_{1}(N)
for some
N
, and
\mathcal{P}_{k}(\mathbb{C})=\bigoplus_{r=1}^{k-1}\mathbb{C}\cdot z^{r-1}
be the space of polynomials in the variable
z
of degree
\leq k-2
with
\mathbb{C}
-coefficients. More generally,
\mathcal{P}_{k}(R)
is defined for any commutative ring
R
in the same way.

As in
[
MTT86
,
\lx@sectionsign
3]
, for

f\in S_{k}
,
P\in\mathcal{P}_{k}(\mathbb{C})
, and
a,m\in\mathbb{Q}
with
m>0
, we define the
modular symbol
by

\lambda(f,z^{r-1},-a,m)=2\pi\sqrt{-1}\cdot\int^{a/m}_{a/m+i\infty}f(z)\cdot(mz-a)^{r-1}dz.

We set

\lambda^{\pm}(f,z^{r-1},a,m)=\lambda(f,z^{r-1};a,m)\pm\lambda(f,z^{r-1},-a,m)
, and
we regard it as a function

\lambda^{\pm}(f,-,-,-):P_{k}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathbb{Q}\times\mathbb{Q}_{>0}\to\mathbb{C}
.
By the work of Manin and Shimura
[
Man72
,
Shi77
]
, there exist periods
\Omega^{\pm}_{f}
such that

\lambda^{\pm}(f,-,-,-):P_{k}(\mathcal{O}_{(\pi)})\times\mathbb{Q}\times\mathbb{Q}_{>0}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{(\pi)}\cdot\Omega^{\pm}_{f}

where
\mathcal{O}_{(\pi)}=\mathcal{O}_{\pi}\cap\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
.
We call these periods the
optimal periods
of
f
.

Remark 3.1
.

(1)

In
[
PW11a
]
, Pollack–Weston defined the cohomological periods of
f
, which are similar to our optimal periods.
Their normalization is equivalent to ours when
\lambda^{\pm}(f,1;a,m)
is considered.

(2)

The optimal periods are determined by
several
values of modular symbols in general, so it seems difficult to deform the optimal periods integrally in
p
-adic families of modular forms. The notion of optimal periods does not require any mod
p
multiplicity one type result.

We define the
optimally normalized modular symbol

by

\lambda^{\pm,\mathrm{opt}}(f,z^{r-1},a,m)=\dfrac{1}{\Omega^{\pm}_{f}}\cdot\left(\lambda(f,z^{r-1},a,m)\pm\lambda(f,z^{r-1},-a,m)\right).

Following
[
MTT86
,
\lx@sectionsign
8]
, the
Mazur–Tate element of
f
over
\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})
at
s=r
is defined by

\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),r}(f):=\sum_{a\in(\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\lambda^{\pm,\mathrm{opt}}(f,z^{r-1},a,m)\cdot\sigma_{a}\in\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})/\mathbb{Q})].

where the sign of modular symbols is that of
(-1)^{r-1}
. See also
[
Ots20
]
.

For
n|m
and a commutative ring
R
,
the
R
-linear map

\pi_{m,n}:R[\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})/\mathbb{Q})]\to R[\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{n})/\mathbb{Q})]

is induced by the natural projection
\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})/\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{n})/\mathbb{Q})

and
the
R
-linear map

\nu_{n,m}:R[\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{n})/\mathbb{Q})]\to R[\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})/\mathbb{Q})]

is defined by

\sigma\mapsto\sum_{\tau\in\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})/\mathbb{Q})}\tau

where
\tau
runs over the elements mapping to
\sigma
under
\pi_{m,n}
.
We identify

\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})/\mathbb{Q})\simeq(\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z})^{\times}

by
\sigma_{a}\mapsto a
and regard a Galois character as a Dirichlet character under this identification.

Proposition 3.2
.

(1)

Let
m
be a positive integer and
\ell
a prime.
Then

\pi_{m\ell,m}\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m\ell}),r}(f)\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\left(a_{\ell}(f)-\ell^{r-1}\cdot\mathrm{Frob}_{\ell}-\psi(\ell)\cdot\ell^{k-1-r}\cdot\mathrm{Frob}^{-1}_{\ell}\right)\cdot\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),r}(f)&(\ell\nmid m)\\
a_{\ell}(f)\cdot\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),r}(f)-\psi(\ell)\cdot\ell^{k-2}\cdot\nu_{m/\ell,m}\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m/\ell}),r}(f)\right)&(\ell\mid m)\end{array}\right.

where
\mathrm{Frob}_{\ell}\in\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})/\mathbb{Q})
is the arithmetic Frobenius at
\ell
.

(2)

For a primitive Dirichlet character
\chi
of conductor
m>1
, we have

\chi\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),r}(f)\right)=m^{r}\cdot(r-1)!\cdot\dfrac{1}{\tau(\chi^{-1})}\cdot\dfrac{L(f,\chi^{-1},r)}{(-2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{r-1}\cdot\Omega^{\pm}_{f}}

where the sign
\pm
is that of
(-1)^{r-1}\cdot\chi(-1)
and
\tau(\chi)={\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma_{a}\in\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})/\mathbb{Q})}}\chi(a)\cdot\zeta^{a}_{m}
is the Gauss sum.

Proof.

See
[
MTT86
,
\lx@sectionsign
4,
\lx@sectionsign
8, and (9.1)]
.
∎

4.
Construction of canonical Kato’s Euler systems

The goal of this section is to construct the
canonical and integral
Kato’s Euler systems for modular forms of
arbitrary
weight at
any odd
prime under the large image assumption.
In
[
Kat04
]
, two
slightly different
cohomology classes are mainly used for arithmetic applications:

(1)

{}_{c,d}z^{(p)}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(f,r,r^{\prime},\xi,S):={}_{c,d}z^{(p)}_{m}(f,r,r^{\prime},\xi,S)\in\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}[1/p,\zeta_{m}]),j_{*}V_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}(f)(k-r))
in
[
Kat04
, (8.1.3) and Ex. 13.3]

(2)

\mathbf{z}^{(p)}_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{H}^{1}(V_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}(f))
where
\gamma\in V_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}(f)
in
[
Kat04
, Thm. 12.5]

For the former one, the tame level Euler system relation is explicitly studied in
[
Kat04
, Prop. 8.12]
; thus, it can be used to bound the Selmer groups via the Euler system argument
[
Kat04
, Theorem 13.4]
. Since this element depends on various choices, it would not give us the exact bound. Especially,
the choices affect the corresponding periods in Kato’s explicit reciprocity law
[
Kat04
, Thm. 6.6]
. Also, the Euler factors at bad primes are ignored.

The latter one is entirely canonical and suitable for the formulation of the Iwasawa main conjecture.
It gives the correct period and remembers the Euler factors at bad primes.
However, it is rather unclear that it satisfies the tame level Euler system relation integrally (c.f.
[
Del08
, Appendix A]
).
It also seems to be related to
[
Rub00
, Wishful Thinking 8.2.2 and Rem. 8.2.5]
.
See also
[
Col04
,
Wan13
]
for a more distribution-theoretic approach.

Our version of Kato’s Euler system works for both purposes. This system of elements
simultaneously
remembers all the Euler factors at bad primes and enjoys the integrality, the tame level Euler system relation, and the correct period.
This choice removes a certain restriction in
[
KKS20
]
, and we give an affirmative answer to
[
KN20
, Ques. A.15]
under the large image assumption. This section is insensitive to the good reduction assumption.

Some part of this section is benefited from the discussion with Takenori Kataoka. See also
[
Kat21
]
for the case of elliptic curves.

4.1.
The cohomology classes and Kato’s explicit reciprocity law

For an integer
A\geq 1
, denote by
\mathrm{prime}(A)
the set of primes dividing
A
.
Let
m\geq 1
be an integer.

Definition 4.1
.

Following
[
Kat04
,
\lx@sectionsign
5.1]
, we define
\xi
and
S
as follows:

(1)

\xi
is a symbol
a(A)
where
a,A\in\mathbb{Z}
,
A\geq 1
and
S
is a non-empty finite set of primes containing
\mathrm{prime}(mA)
, or

(2)

\xi
is an element of
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
and
S
is a non-empty finite set of primes containing
\mathrm{prime}(mN)
.

Definition 4.2
.

Following
[
Kat04
, (5.2.1) and (5.2.2)]
, we define integers
r,r^{\prime}
and positive integers
c,d
as follows:

(1)

1\leq r\leq k-1
,
1\leq r^{\prime}\leq k-1
, and at least one of
r
,
r^{\prime}
is
k-1
.

(2)

c
and
d
are positive integers with
\mathrm{prime}(cd)\cap S=\emptyset
, and
(d,N)=1
.

Write

\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Z}[1/p,\zeta_{m}],V_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}(f)(k-r))=\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{m},1/p]),j_{*}V_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}(f)(k-r))

where
j:\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}))\to\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{m},1/p])
is the natural map.
Denote by

{}_{c,d}z^{(p)}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(f,r,r^{\prime},\xi,S)={}_{c,d}z^{(p)}_{m}(f,r,r^{\prime},\xi,S)\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Z}[1/p,\zeta_{m}],V_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}(f)(k-r))

the (imprimitive) zeta element in
[
Kat04
, (8.1.3)]

where
p\in S
as well as all the “variables” satisfy Definition
4.1
and Definition
4.2
.
By
[
Kat04
, Prop. 8.12]
, the cohomology classes

{}_{c,d}z^{(p)}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(f,r,r^{\prime},\xi,S)\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Z}[1/p,\zeta_{m}],V_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}(f)(k-r))

satisfy the tame level Euler system relation except at primes dividing
cdN
. See also
[
Kat04
, Ex. 13.3]
.
Denote by

{}_{c,d}z_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(f,r,r^{\prime},\xi,S)={}_{c,d}z_{m}(f,r,r^{\prime},\xi,S)\in S(f)\otimes\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})

the zeta modular form in
[
Kat04
,
\lx@sectionsign
6.3]

where
c
,
d
,
r
,
r^{\prime}
,
\xi
, and
S
satisfy Definition
4.1
and Definition
4.2
.
By
[
Kat04
, Thm. 9.7]
, we have the assignment

{}_{c,d}z^{(p)}_{m}(f,r,r^{\prime},\xi,S)\mapsto{}_{c,d}z_{m}(f,r,r^{\prime},\xi,S)

under the dual exponential map.

Theorem 4.3

(Kato’s explicit reciprocity law)
.

Let
\chi
be a character on
(\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
.
Let
\pm=(-1)^{k-r-1}\cdot\chi(-1)
and

(u,v):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(r+2-k,r)&\textrm{if }r^{\prime}=k-1\\
(k-r^{\prime},r^{\prime})&\textrm{if }r=k-1\end{array}\right.

following
[
Kat04
, (4.2.4)]
.
We add the following condition.

(
*
)

If
\xi\in\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
, then assume
c\equiv d\equiv 1\ (\text{mod}\ N)
.

Then we have

\sum_{b\in(\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\chi(b)\cdot\mathrm{per}_{f}\left(\sigma_{b}\left({}_{c,d}z_{m}(f,r,r^{\prime},\xi,S)\right)\right)^{\pm}=L^{(S)}(f,\chi,r)\cdot(2\pi i)^{k-r-1}\cdot\gamma^{\pm}

where

\mathrm{per}_{f}
is Kato’s period map in
[
Kat04
,
\lx@sectionsign
6.3]
,

\displaystyle\gamma=c^{2}d^{2}\delta(f,r^{\prime},a(A))

\displaystyle-c^{u}d^{2}\overline{\chi}(c)\delta(f,r^{\prime},ac(A))

\displaystyle-c^{2}d^{v}\psi(d)\delta(f,r^{\prime},``a/d"(A))+c^{u}d^{v}\overline{\chi}(cd)\psi(d)\delta(f,r^{\prime},``ac/d"(A))

if
\xi=a(A)
, and

\gamma=(c^{2}-c^{u}\overline{\chi}(c))(d^{2}-d^{v}\overline{\chi}(d))\delta(f,r^{\prime},\xi)

if
\xi\in\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
.

Proof.

See
[
Kat04
, Thm. 6.6]
.
∎

4.2.
The module of primitive zeta elements

Following
\lx@sectionsign
2.3
,
write

\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),V_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}(f)(k-r))=\varprojlim_{F}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{F}[1/p]),j_{*}V_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}(f)(k-r))

where
F
runs over finite extensions of
\mathbb{Q}
in
\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{\infty}})
.
The following definition comes from
[
Kat04
, Thm. 12.6]
,
[
Del08
, Appendix A]
, and
[
Kat21
, Thm. 6.1]
.

Definition 4.4
.

We define be the
module of imprimitive zeta elements

\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(f,k-r)\subseteq\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),V_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}(f)(k-r))

by the submodule generated by

{}_{c,d}\mathbf{z}^{(p)}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(f,k-r,j,a(A),\mathrm{prime}(pmA)):=\varprojlim_{n}\left({}_{c,d}z^{(p)}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})}(f,k-r,j,a(A),\mathrm{prime}(pmA))\otimes(\zeta_{p^{n}})^{\otimes k-r}\right)

where
1\leq j\leq k-1
,
a,A\in\mathbb{Z}
with
A\geq 1
,
c,d\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
(c,6pmA)=(d,6pmN)=1
, and

{}_{c,d}\mathbf{z}^{(p)}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(f,k-r,j,\alpha,\mathrm{prime}(pmN)):=\varprojlim_{n}\left({}_{c,d}z^{(p)}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})}(f,k-r,j,\alpha,\mathrm{prime}(pmN))\otimes(\zeta_{p^{n}})^{\otimes k-r}\right)

where
1\leq j\leq k-1
,
\alpha\in\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
,
c,d\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
(cd,6pmN)=1
and
c\equiv d\equiv 1\pmod{N}
.

Choice 4.5
.

Fix elements
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
and integers
j_{1},j_{2}
such that

•

1\leq j_{i}\leq k-1

(i=1,2)
, and

•

\delta(f,j_{1},\alpha_{1})^{+}\neq 0
and
\delta(f,j_{2},\alpha_{2})^{-}\neq 0
(
[
Kat04
, (13.6)]
).

For a non-zero
\gamma\in V_{F_{\pi}}(f)
, we write

\gamma=b_{1}\cdot\delta(f,j_{1},\alpha_{1})^{+}+b_{2}\cdot\delta(f,j_{2},\alpha_{2})^{-}\neq 0

for some
b_{1},b_{2}\in F_{\pi}
.

Choice 4.6
.

Let
m\geq 1
be an integer.
Fix
c,d\in\mathbb{Z}
such that

•

(cd,6pm)=1
,

•

c\equiv d\equiv 1\ (\text{mod}\ N)
(needed for
[
Kat04
, Thm. 6.6]
), and

•

c^{2}\neq 1
and
d^{2}\neq 1
.

For a commutative ring
R
, let
Q(R)
be the total quotient ring of
R
.
Let
G_{mp^{\infty}}=\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{\infty}})/\mathbb{Q})
and
\mathcal{O}_{\pi}\llbracket G_{mp^{\infty}}\rrbracket=\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}w}[\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})/\mathbb{Q})]
.

Definition 4.7
.

For each
\gamma\in V_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}(f)
, we define the
primitive zeta element
by

\displaystyle\mathbf{z}^{(p)}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\gamma}:=

\displaystyle\left\{\mu(c,d,j_{1})^{-1}\cdot b_{1}\cdot{}_{c,d}\mathbf{z}^{(p)}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(f,k,j_{1},\alpha_{1},\mathrm{prime}(pmN))\right\}^{-}

\displaystyle+\left\{\mu(c,d,j_{2})^{-1}\cdot b_{2}\cdot{}_{c,d}\mathbf{z}^{(p)}_{\mathbb{Q}(\mu_{m})}(f,k,j_{2},\alpha_{2},\mathrm{prime}(pmN))\right\}^{+}

in
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),V_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}(f))\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}\llbracket G_{mp^{\infty}}\rrbracket}Q(\mathcal{O}_{\pi}\llbracket G_{mp^{\infty}}\rrbracket)

where

\mu(c,d,j):=(c^{2}-c^{k+1-j}\cdot\sigma_{c})\cdot(d^{2}-d^{j+1}\cdot\sigma_{d})\cdot\prod_{\ell\mid N}(1-a_{\ell}(f)\ell^{-k}\sigma^{-1}_{\ell})\in\mathcal{O}_{\pi}\llbracket G_{mp^{\infty}}\rrbracket

for
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and all the choices in the RHS satisfy Choice
4.5
and Choice
4.6
.
However,
\mathbf{z}^{(p)}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\gamma}
itself is independent of Choice
4.5
and Choice
4.6
.
Note that
\mu(c,d,j)
is not a zero divisor in
\mathcal{O}_{\pi}\llbracket G_{mp^{\infty}}\rrbracket
for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Here, the primitivity means that the Euler factors at bad reduction primes are built in Kato’s explicit reciprocity law.
It is not related to the primitivity of Kolyvagin systems.

Definition 4.8
.

For any Galois-stable lattice
T_{f}\subseteq V_{F_{\pi}}(f)
,
we define the
module of primitive zeta elements
by the submodule

\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(T_{f}(k-r))\subseteq\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),T_{f}(k-r))\otimes Q(\mathcal{O}_{\pi}\llbracket G_{mp^{\infty}}\rrbracket)

generated by

\mathbf{z}^{(p)}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\gamma}\otimes\varprojlim_{n}\left((\zeta_{p^{n}})^{\otimes k-r}\right)

where
\gamma
runs over
T_{f}(k-r)
.

A slight generalization of
[
Kat04
, Lem. 13.10]
from
\mathbb{Q}
to
\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})
(e.g.
[
Del08
, Fact
\#
1 and Fact
\#
2 in Appendix A]
) yields that

(4.1)

\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(f,k-r)\subseteq\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(V_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}(f)(k-r)).

By
[
Del08
, Key Claim in Appendix A]
, which generalizes
[
Kat04
,
\lx@sectionsign
13.12]
,
the index of (
4.1
) is finite; thus, we have

\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(V_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}(f)(k-r))\subseteq\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),V_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}(f)(k-r))\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}.

We have the following picture up to now

\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(f,k-r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
fin. index
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(V_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}(f)(k-r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{?}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),V_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}(f)(k-r))}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),V_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}(f)(k-r))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}.}

Proposition 4.9
.

If
\rho_{f}
has large image, then we have

\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(T_{f}(k-r))\subseteq\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),T_{f}(k-r))

where
T_{f}
is any Galois-stable lattice of
V_{F_{\pi}}(f)
.

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
2.2
and
[
Kat04
,
\lx@sectionsign
13.14]
.
∎

4.3.
Canonical Kato’s Euler systems

We keep the large image assumption on
\rho_{f}
in this subsection.

Definition 4.10
.

(1)

For each
\gamma\in T_{f}
, we define the
canonical Kato’s Euler system over the cyclotomic tower
by

\mathbf{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\gamma}(f,k-r):=\mathbf{z}^{(p)}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\gamma}\otimes\varprojlim_{n}\left((\zeta_{p^{n}})^{\otimes k-r}\right)\in\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),T_{f}(k-r)).

(2)

For each
\gamma\in T_{f}
, we define the
canonical Kato’s Euler system

z_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}}),\gamma}(f,k-r)\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{mp^{n}},1/p],T_{f}(k-r))

by the image of
\mathbf{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\gamma}(f,k-r)

under the corestriction map

\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),T_{f}(k-r))\to\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{mp^{n}},1/p],T_{f}(k-r)).

Let
\gamma^{+}_{0}\in T^{+}_{f}
and
\gamma^{-}_{0}\in T^{-}_{f}
be
\mathcal{O}_{\pi}
-generators of
T^{+}_{f}
and
T^{-}_{f}
, respectively, and write
\gamma_{0}=\gamma^{+}_{0}+\gamma^{-}_{0}\in T_{f}
.
Write
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(T_{f}(k-r))=\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(\mathbb{Q},T_{f}(k-r))
for convenience.

Proposition 4.11
.

If
\rho_{f}
has large image, then
the submodule

\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{prim}}(T_{f}(k-r)):=\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{\mathbb{Q}}(T_{f}(k-r))\subseteq\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(T_{f}(k-r))

is generated by
\mathbf{z}_{\mathbb{Q},\gamma_{0}}(f,k-r)
over
\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}w}
.

Proof.

See
[
KN20
, Prop. A.12]
.
∎

Definition
4.10
yields a generalization of Kato’s zeta morphism
T_{f}\to\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(T_{f})
defined by
\gamma\mapsto\mathbf{z}^{(p)}_{\gamma}
in
[
Kat04
, Thm. 12.5.(1)]
by adding tamely ramified abelian extensions.

Proposition 4.12
.

Let
\ell
be a prime not equal to
Np
and
\gamma\in T_{f}
.
The corestriction map induced from the norm map from
\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m\ell p^{n}})
to
\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})

\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{m\ell p^{n}},1/p],T_{f}(k-r))\to\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{mp^{n}},1/p],T_{f}(k-r))

sends
z_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m\ell p^{n}}),\gamma}(f,k-r)
to

\left(1-a_{\ell}(\overline{f})\cdot\mathrm{Frob}^{-1}_{\ell}\cdot\ell^{-r}+\overline{\psi}(\ell)\cdot\mathrm{Frob}^{-2}_{\ell}\cdot\ell^{k-1-2r}\right)\cdot z_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}}),\gamma}(f,k-r)

where
\mathrm{Frob}_{\ell}
is the arithmetic Frobenius at
\ell
in
\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})/\mathbb{Q})
.

Proof.

It immediately follows from
[
Kat04
, Prop. 8.12]
.
∎

Let
\gamma=\gamma^{+}+\gamma^{-}\in V_{f}
and
z_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}}),\gamma}(f,k-r)\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{mp^{n}},1/p],V_{f}(k-r))
be the canonical Kato’s Euler system with respect to
\gamma
.
Write

\mathrm{exp}^{*}\left(\mathrm{loc}_{p}\left(z_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}}),\gamma}(f,k-r)\right)\right)=c_{k-r,mp^{n},\gamma}\cdot\omega_{f}

where

\mathrm{exp}^{*}=\mathrm{exp}^{*}_{V_{f}(k-r),\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})}=\sum_{v}\mathrm{exp}^{*}_{V_{f}(k-r),\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})_{v}}

is the sum of the Bloch–Kato dual exponential maps at
v
a prime of
\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})
dividing
p
,

c_{k-r,mp^{n},\gamma}\in\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})\otimes F_{\pi}
, and

\omega_{f}
is the basis of
S(f)
induced from the
normalized
eigenform
f
.

Following the convention of Kato’s period map
\mathrm{per}_{f}

[
Kat04
,
\lx@sectionsign
7.6, (7.13.6) and Theorem 16.2]
,
Kato’s periods are defined by

\mathrm{per}_{f}(\omega_{f})=\Omega^{+}_{\gamma,\omega_{f}}\cdot\gamma^{+}+\Omega^{-}_{\gamma,\omega_{f}}\cdot\gamma^{-}.

Theorem 4.13
.

For a primitive Dirichlet character
\chi
of conductor
mp^{n}
with
(m,Np)=1
, we have the interpolation formula

\sum_{b\in(\mathbb{Z}/mp^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\chi(b)\cdot\sigma_{b}\left(c_{k-r,mp^{n},\gamma}\right)\cdot\omega_{f}=(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{k-r-1}\cdot\dfrac{L^{(p)}(\overline{f},\chi,r)}{\Omega^{\pm}_{\gamma,\omega_{f}}}\cdot\omega_{f}

where

L^{(p)}(\overline{f},\chi,r)
is the
p
-imprimitive
L
-value of
\overline{f}
at
s=r
twisted by
\chi
,

\Omega^{\pm}_{\gamma,\omega_{f}}
is Kato’s period, and the sign of Kato’s period coincides with the sign of
(-1)^{k-r-1}\cdot\chi(-1)
.

Proof.

It immediately follows from Theorem
4.3
and the definition of canonical Kato’s Euler systems.
∎

Comparing the conventions between Kato’s periods
[
Kat04
,
\lx@sectionsign
7.6]
and Mazur–Tate–Teitelbaum’s ones
[
MTT86
,
\lx@sectionsign
1 and
\lx@sectionsign
7]
, we obtain
