C^{\pm}_{\mathrm{per}}\cdot\Omega^{\pm}_{\gamma,\omega_{f}}=-(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{k-2}\cdot\Omega^{\pm}_{\overline{f}}

where

•

C^{\pm}_{\mathrm{per}}\in\mathcal{O}_{(\pi)}
is uniquely determined up to
\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{(\pi)}
, respectively,

•

(the sign of
C^{\pm}_{\mathrm{per}}
) = (the sign of
\Omega^{\pm}_{\gamma,\omega_{f}}
) = (the sign of
\Omega^{\pm}_{\overline{f}}
) if
k
is even, and

•

(the sign of
C^{\pm}_{\mathrm{per}}
) = (the sign of
\Omega^{\mp}_{\gamma,\omega_{f}}
) = (the sign of
\Omega^{\pm}_{\overline{f}}
) if
k
is odd.

Let
\delta^{\pm}_{f}\in V^{\pm}_{F}(f)
be non-zero elements determined by the relation

(4.2)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathrm{per}_{f}(\omega_{f})&=\gamma^{+}\otimes\Omega^{+}_{\gamma,\omega_{f}}+\gamma^{-}\otimes\Omega^{-}_{\gamma,\omega_{f}}\\
&=\delta^{+}_{f}\otimes C^{\pm}_{\mathrm{per}}\cdot\Omega^{+}_{\gamma,\omega_{f}}+\delta^{-}_{f}\otimes C^{\mp}_{\mathrm{per}}\cdot\Omega^{-}_{\gamma,\omega_{f}}.\end{split}

where the sign convention of
C^{\pm}_{\mathrm{per}}
follows the above.

For our purpose, we modify the Euler system
\left\{z_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}}),\gamma}(f,k-r)\right\}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})}
in the sense of “Varying the Euler factors”
[
Rub00
,
\lx@sectionsign
9.6]
.

Corollary 4.14
.

For each
\gamma\in T_{f}
,
there exists a system of cohomology classes

\left\{\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}}),\gamma}(f,k-r)\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{mp^{n}},1/p],T_{f}(k-r))\right\}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})}

such that

(1)

\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m\ell p^{n}}),\gamma}(f,k-r)
maps to

\left(1-a_{\ell}(\overline{f})\cdot\mathrm{Frob}^{-1}_{\ell}\cdot\ell^{1-r}+\overline{\psi}(\ell)\cdot\mathrm{Frob}^{-2}_{\ell}\cdot\ell^{k-2-2r}\right)\cdot\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}}),\gamma}(f,k-r)

under the corestriction map in Theorem
4.12
,

(2)

\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}}),\gamma}(f,k-r)=z_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}}),\gamma}(f,k-r)
, and

(3)

if we choose the optimal period
\gamma=\delta_{f}\in V_{f}
, then we have

\sum_{b\in(\mathbb{Z}/mp^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\chi(b)\cdot\mathrm{exp}^{*}\left(\mathrm{loc}_{p}\left(\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}}),\delta_{f}}(f,k-r)^{\sigma_{b}}\right)\right)=-\dfrac{L^{(p)}(\overline{f},\chi,r)}{(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{r-1}\cdot\Omega^{\pm}_{\overline{f}}}\cdot\omega_{f}

under the same setting in Theorem
4.13

where
\pm
is the sign of
(-1)^{r-1}\cdot\chi(-1)
.

Proof.

It is a simple application of
[
Rub00
, Lem. 9.6.1]
.
∎

The
modified Kato’s Euler system over the cyclotomic tower
is defined by

\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\gamma}(f,k-r):=\varprojlim_{n}\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}}),\gamma}(f,k-r)

where
\gamma\in V_{f}
, and we have
\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathbb{Q},\gamma}(f,k-r)=\mathbf{z}_{\mathbb{Q},\gamma}(f,k-r)
.

The following Iwasawa main conjecture without
p
-adic
L
-functions is due to Kato
[
Kat04
, Conj. 12.10]
.

Conjecture 4.15

(Kato’s main conjecture)
.

Let
i\in\{0,\cdots,p-2\}
.
Let
\mathbf{z}_{\mathbb{Q},\gamma_{0}}(f,i,k-r)\in\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f,i}(k-r))
be Kato’s zeta element for
T_{f,i}(k-r)
over the cyclotomic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-extension of
\mathbb{Q}
where
r\in\mathbb{Z}
.
If
\rho_{f}
has large image, then

(4.3)

\mathrm{char}_{\Lambda^{(i)}_{\mathrm{Iw}}}\dfrac{\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f,i}(k-r))}{\Lambda^{(i)}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\mathbf{z}_{\mathbb{Q},\gamma_{0}}(f,i,k-r)}=\mathrm{char}_{\Lambda^{(i)}_{\mathrm{Iw}}}\mathrm{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f,i}(k-r))

as ideals of
\Lambda^{(i)}_{\mathrm{Iw}}
.

We say that the
main conjecture for
T_{f,i}(k-r)
holds
if (
4.3
) holds for the fixed
i
. It is easy to see that Conjecture
4.15
for all
i
is independent of
r
9
9

9

However, Tate twist changes
i
; for example, the statements for

\mathrm{H}^{a}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f,i}(k-r))
are equivalent to those for
\mathrm{H}^{a}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f,i+r^{\prime}-r}(k-r^{\prime}))
. See
[
Rub00
,
\lx@sectionsign
6.5]
for detail.
.

Theorem 4.16

(Kato)
.

One inclusion
\subseteq
in (
4.3
) holds.

Proof.

See
[
Kat04
, Thm. 12.5.(4)]
and
[
MR04
, Thm. 5.3.10.(i)]
.
∎

We also concern a slightly generalized form of Conjecture A of Coates–Sujatha
[
CS05
, Conj. A]
.

Conjecture 4.17

(Coates–Sujatha)
.

For each
i=0,\cdots,p-2
, the finitely generated torsion
\Lambda^{(i)}_{\mathrm{Iw}}
-module
\mathrm{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f,i}(k-r))
is indeed a finitely generated
\mathcal{O}_{\pi}
-module. In other words,

\mu\left(\mathrm{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f,i}(k-r))\right)=0.

5.
Review of
p
-adic Hodge theory

We quickly review the basic notion of
p
-adic Hodge theory emphasizing its
integral
aspect via the theory of Wach modules.
Especially, we focus on Benois’
\varphi
-stable integral lattice in the crystalline Dieudonné module, which gives us the relevant integrality.
The existence of such a lattice is essential for the construction of the compatible families of the
integral
local points in
\lx@sectionsign
7
.
Although no new result is presented in this section, the content and the convention of this section play important roles in proving the integrality of the finite layer Coleman maps.
We closely follow
[
Ben00
,
\lx@sectionsign
1]
.

5.1.
Rings of
p
-adic periods

We review the rings of
p
-adic periods à la Fontaine. See
[
Ber04
,
Car19
]
for details.
For convenience, we record both notations due to Colmez and Fontaine.

Let
K/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
be a finite
unramified
extension and
W=\mathcal{O}_{K}
with finite residue field
\mathbb{F}
and
C=\widehat{\overline{K}}
the
p
-adic completion of an algebraic closure of
K
.
Let
v:C\to\mathbb{R}\cup\{\infty\}
be the valuation of
C
normalized by
v(p)=1
.
Let

\mathcal{R}=\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{+}:={\displaystyle\varprojlim_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathcal{O}_{C}/p\mathcal{O}_{C}}
and
\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}=\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{+}:=W(\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{+})

where
W(R)
is the ring of Witt vector of (perfect) ring
R
.

An element
x\in\mathcal{R}
can be viewed as a sequence
(x_{n})_{n\geq 0}
such that
x^{p}_{n+1}=x_{n}
for all
n\geq 0
.
Let
\widehat{x}_{n}
be any lifting of
x_{n}
in
\mathcal{O}_{C}
, and then the sequence
\widehat{x}^{p^{n}}_{m+n}
converges to some
x^{(m)}
in
\mathcal{O}_{C}
and it is independent of the choice of liftings.
The ring
\mathcal{R}
equipped with the valuation
v_{\mathcal{R}}(x)=v(x^{(0)})
is a complete local ring of characteristic
p
with residue field
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
. Furthermore,
\mathcal{R}
is integrally closed in
\mathrm{Frac}(\mathcal{R})
.

Choice 5.1
.

We fix a generator
\varepsilon=\left(\zeta_{p^{n}}\right)_{n\geq 0}
of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)
and denote by the same letter the unique element
\varepsilon\in\mathcal{R}
such that
\varepsilon^{(n)}=\zeta_{p^{n}}
.

Then
W(\mathcal{R})
and
W(\mathrm{Frac}(\mathcal{R}))
are complete rings of characteristic zero containing
W(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
. Moreover, they are endowed with the action of the Galois group
G_{K}
and the Frobenius
\varphi
. Let

[-]:\mathcal{R}\to W(\mathcal{R})

be the Teichmüller lifting.
Then any
u=(u_{n})_{n\geq 0}\in W(\mathcal{R})
can be written as

u=\sum_{m\geq 0}[u^{p^{-m}}_{m}]\cdot p^{m}
.

Put
\pi=[\varepsilon]-1\in W(\mathcal{R})
and
\pi_{n}=\varphi^{-n}(\pi)=[\varepsilon]^{1/p^{n}}-1\in W(\mathcal{R})
for
n\geq 0
; thus,
\pi=\pi_{0}
.
Let

S_{n}=W\llbracket\pi_{n}\rrbracket\subseteq W(\mathcal{R})

be the
W
-subring of
W(\mathcal{R})
generated by
\pi_{n}
. Write
S=S_{0}
. Then
S_{n}
is stable under the action of
G_{K}
and
\varphi
; more explicitly, we have

\textstyle{g(\pi_{n})=(1+\pi_{n})^{\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}(g)}-1,}
\textstyle{\varphi(\pi_{n})=(1+\pi_{n})^{p}-1}

where
g\in G_{K}
and
\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}:G_{K}\to\mathrm{Gal}(K(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})/K)\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}
is the
p
-adic cyclotomic character.

Since
\pi_{n}
is invertible in
W(\mathrm{Frac}(\mathcal{R}))
, we regard
S_{n}[\pi^{-1}_{n}]
as a subring of
W(\mathrm{Frac}(\mathcal{R}))
.
We write

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E},\pi_{n}}=\widehat{S_{n}[\pi^{-1}_{n}]}=\left\{\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}a_{k}\pi^{k}_{n}:a_{k}\in W,a_{k}\to 0\textrm{ as }k\to-\infty\right\}

where
\widehat{(-)}
means the
p
-adic completion (the strong topology).
When
n=0
, we also write
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E},\pi}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
.
Denote by
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\mathcal{E},\pi_{n}}
the maximal unramified extension of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E},\pi_{n}}
and by
\widehat{\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\mathcal{E},\pi_{n}}}
the
p
-adic completion of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\mathcal{E},\pi_{n}}
.
Denote by

S^{\mathrm{PD}}=S\left[\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{2!},\dfrac{\pi^{3}}{3!},\cdots\right]

the PD-envelope of
S

and
I^{\langle i\rangle}
the ideal of
S^{\mathrm{PD}}
generated by
\dfrac{\pi^{m}}{m!}
,
m\geq i
.
Write

\widehat{S^{\mathrm{PD}}}=\varprojlim_{i}S^{\mathrm{PD}}/I^{\langle i\rangle}
.

Let

\theta:\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}=\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{+}=W(\mathcal{R})\to\mathcal{O}_{C}

be the map given by the formula

\theta\left(\sum_{m\geq 0}[u_{m}]\cdot p^{m}\right)=\sum_{m\geq 0}u^{(0)}_{m}\cdot p^{m}
.
Then
\theta
is a surjective ring homomorphism and
\mathrm{ker}\ \theta
is generated by

\omega=\sum_{i=0}^{p-1}[\varepsilon]^{i/p}=\dfrac{[\varepsilon]-1}{[\varepsilon]^{1/p}-1}

where
[\varepsilon]^{1/p}=[(\varepsilon^{(1)},\cdots)]
.
By inverting
p
, we extend
\theta
to

\theta:\mathbf{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{inf}}=\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{+}=W(\mathcal{R})[1/p]\to C

and define

\mathbf{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}:=\varprojlim_{n}\mathbf{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{inf}}/\left(\mathrm{ker}\ \theta\right)^{n}
.
It is known that
\mathbf{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
is a complete discrete valuation ring with residue field
C
and admits
G_{K}
-action.
Since
\theta(\pi)=\theta([\varepsilon]-1)=0
,
[\varepsilon]-1
is “small” with respect to the topology of
\mathbf{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
.
Thus, the series

\mathrm{log}([\varepsilon])=\sum_{m\geq 1}^{\infty}(-1)^{m+1}\dfrac{\pi^{m}}{m}=-\sum_{m\geq 1}^{\infty}\dfrac{(1-[\varepsilon])^{m}}{m}

converges to the “
p
-adic
2\pi i
”
t
in
\mathbf{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
with respect to the
(\mathrm{ker}\ \theta)
-adic topology.
The element
t
also generates the maximal ideal of
\mathbf{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
and
G_{K}
acts on
t
by
g\cdot t=\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}(g)\cdot t
.
The field of fractions of
\mathbf{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
is denoted by
\mathbf{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}
, and
\mathbf{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}
is the complete discrete valuation field equipped with
G_{K}
-action and the filtration given by
t
.

Let

\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{cris}}:=\widehat{\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}\left[\dfrac{\omega^{2}}{2!},\dfrac{\omega^{3}}{3!},\cdots\right]}
be
the
p
-adic completion of the PD-envelope of
\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}
with respect to
\mathrm{ker}\ \theta
.
It is easy to see
t\in\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{cris}}
.
We define

\mathbf{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{cris}}:=\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{cris}}[1/p]
and

\mathbf{B}_{\mathrm{cris}}:=\mathbf{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{cris}}[1/t]=\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{cris}}[1/p,1/t]
.
It is known that
t
lies in
\mathbf{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
and
\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{cris}}
.
Due to
[
Ben00
,
\lx@sectionsign
1.1.2]
,
\widehat{S^{\mathrm{PD}}}
embeds in
\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{cris}}
.

5.2.
The classification of Galois representations via
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules

Let
K_{n}=K(\zeta_{p^{n}})
and
K_{\infty}=\cup_{n\geq 1}K_{n}
.
We recall Fontaine’s theorem on the classification of continuous representations of
G_{K_{n}}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
in terms of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules
[
Fon90
]
.

Let
\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(G_{K_{n}})
be the category of finite
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-modules equipped with a continuous linear action of
G_{K_{n}}
and
M^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}_{\varphi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E},\pi_{n}})
the category of étale
(\varphi,\Gamma_{n})
-modules over
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E},\pi_{n}}
where
\Gamma_{n}=\mathrm{Gal}(K_{\infty}/K_{n})
.

Theorem 5.2

(Fontaine)
.

The functor

\textstyle{\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E},\pi_{n}}}:\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(G_{K_{n}})\to M^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}_{\varphi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E},\pi_{n}}),}
\textstyle{T\mapsto\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E},\pi_{n}}}(T)=\left(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\mathcal{E},\pi_{n}}}\right)^{G_{K_{\infty}}}}

is an equivalence of categories.
The functor

\textstyle{\mathbf{V}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E},\pi_{n}}}:M^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}_{\varphi,\Gamma}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E},\pi_{n}})\to\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(G_{K_{n}}),}
\textstyle{M\mapsto\mathbf{V}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E},\pi_{n}}}(M)=\left(M\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E},\pi_{n}}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\mathcal{E},\pi_{n}}}\right)^{\varphi=1}}

is a quasi-inverse to
\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E},\pi_{n}}}
.

Proof.

See
[
Fon90
, Thm. 3.4.3 and Rem. 3.4.4.(b)]
.
More specifically, we take
L=K_{n}
and
K=K_{0}
since
K
is unramified in
[
Fon90
, Rem. 3.4.4.(b)]
.
∎

5.3.
The classification of crystalline representations via Wach modules

We construct an integral lattice of
\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)
in terms of Wach modules.
Let

\widetilde{S}_{n}:=\widehat{\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\mathcal{E},\pi_{n}}}\cap\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}=\widehat{\left(\widehat{S_{n}[\pi^{-1}_{n}]}^{\mathrm{ur}}\right)}\cap\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}

for
n\geq 0
and say
\widetilde{S}=\widetilde{S}_{0}
.
Let
T
be a free
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module of finite rank endowed with continuous linear action of
G_{K_{n}}
.
We define

\mathbf{D}_{S_{n}}(T):=\left(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\widetilde{S}_{n}\right)^{G_{K_{\infty}}}

and

\mathbf{D}_{S}(T):=\left(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\widetilde{S}\right)^{G_{K_{\infty}}}
.
Then we have

\mathbf{D}_{S}(T)=\varphi^{n}\mathbf{D}_{S_{n}}(T)

and

\mathbf{D}_{S_{n}}(T)
is a free
S_{n}
-module with natural actions of
\varphi
and
\Gamma_{n}
.
One has

\mathrm{rk}_{S_{n}}\mathbf{D}_{S_{n}}(T)\leq\mathrm{rk}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E},\pi_{n}}}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E},\pi_{n}}}(T)=\mathrm{rk}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(T)
.

We say that, as a representation of
G_{K_{n}}
,
T
is a
representation of finite height
if
\mathrm{rk}_{S_{n}}\mathbf{D}_{S_{n}}(T)=\mathrm{rk}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(T)
.

Theorem 5.3

(Colmez)
.

Any crystalline representation of
G_{K}
is of finite height.

Proof.

It follows from
[
Col99
]
since
K
is unramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.
∎

Theorem 5.4

(Wach)
.

Let
T
be a representation of finite height.
Then the following statements are equivalent:

(1)

V=T\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is crystalline as a representation of
G_{K_{n}}
.

(2)

There exists a free
S_{n}
-submodule
N\subseteq\mathbf{D}_{S_{n}}(T)
of rank
d
such that
\Gamma_{n}
acts trivially on
(N/\pi N)(-h)
for some
h\in\mathbb{Z}
.

The integer
h
in Theorem
5.4
satisfies
\mathbf{D}^{-h}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)=\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)
as in
[
Ben00
,
\lx@sectionsign
4.1.2]
. See also Assumption
6.2
.
The lattice we want to construct naturally arises in the proof of
(2)\Rightarrow(1)
in Theorem
5.4
.
See
[
Ben00
, Page 220–221]
,
[
Wac96
]
for details.
Let
N_{S}
be the
N
in Theorem
5.4
when
n=0
.
Since

\left(N_{S}\widehat{\otimes}_{S}\widehat{S^{\mathrm{PD}}}\right)^{\Gamma_{n}}\subseteq\left(\mathbf{D}_{S}(T)\widehat{\otimes}_{S}\widehat{S^{\mathrm{PD}}}\right)^{\Gamma_{n}}\subseteq(T\otimes\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{cris}})^{G_{K_{n}}}
,
the
W
-module

(5.1)

M:=\left(\mathbf{D}_{S}(T)\widehat{\otimes}_{S}\widehat{S^{\mathrm{PD}}}\right)^{\Gamma_{n}}\subseteq\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)

is a
\varphi
-stable lattice of
\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)
. This is the lattice we need for the construction of equivariant interal local points.
Another description of the lattice
M
can also be found in
[
BB08
,
\lx@sectionsign
3.2]
.

6.
Local Iwasawa theory and explicit reciprocity law à la Perrin-Riou

We review local Iwasawa theory, the big exponential map, and the explicit reciprocity law developed by Perrin-Riou
[
PR94
]
and its refinement due to Benois and Berger
[
Ben00
,
Ber03
,
BB08
,
Ben14
]
.
Our description keeps the
integrality
information as much as possible; as a consequence, we are able to construct
equivariant

p
-adic
L
-functions of modular forms with the precise denominator information.
The integrality in
[
PR94
]
is based on Fontaine–Laffaille modules as described in
[
PR94
,
\lx@sectionsign
2.1.5 and 2.2.1.Prop. (iv)]
, but the integrality here comes from Wach modules following
[
Ben00
]
.

6.1.
Rings of power series with (
\varphi,\Gamma
)-action

For
h\geq 1
and a subfield
L\subseteq\mathbb{C}_{p}
, we put

\mathscr{H}_{h,L}=\left\{\sum_{n\geq 0}a_{n}X^{n}\in L\llbracket X\rrbracket:\lim_{n\to\infty}\dfrac{|a_{n}|_{p}}{n^{h}}=0\right\}

and

\mathscr{H}_{L}
is the ring of convergent power series over
L
on the open unit ball.

Let
K/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
be a finite unramified extension and consider
\mathscr{H}_{K}\subseteq K\llbracket X\rrbracket
.
Let
\sigma:K\to K
be the absolute Frobenius map on
K
and extend it to
\mathscr{H}_{K}
by

\sigma\left(\sum_{n\geq 0}a_{n}X^{n}\right)=\sum_{n\geq 0}a^{\sigma}_{n}X^{n}
.
We define the Frobenius map on
\varphi:\mathscr{H}_{K}\to\mathscr{H}_{K}

by

\varphi\left(\sum_{n\geq 0}a_{n}X^{n}\right)=\sum_{n\geq 0}a^{\sigma}_{n}\left(\left(1+X\right)^{p}-1\right)^{n}
.
By abuse of notation, denote by
\varphi
the Frobenius map
\varphi\otimes\varphi
on

\mathscr{H}_{K}\otimes_{K}\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)

where
V
is a crystalline representation of
G_{K}
.
Also,
\mathrm{Gal}(K_{\infty}/K)
acts on
\mathscr{H}_{K}
by

\gamma\left(\sum_{n\geq 0}a_{n}X^{n}\right)=\sum_{n\geq 0}a_{n}\left(\left(1+X\right)^{\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}(\gamma)}-1\right)^{n}

where
\gamma\in\mathrm{Gal}(K_{\infty}/K)
and
\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}
is the
p
-adic cyclotomic character.

Let
W\subseteq K
be the ring of integers and the action of
\varphi
and
\mathrm{Gal}(K_{\infty}/K)
on
\mathscr{H}_{K}
can be restricted to
W\llbracket X\rrbracket
.
Also, the Frobenius map
\varphi
(abusing notation again) on
W\llbracket X\rrbracket\otimes M
is defined by
\varphi\otimes\varphi
where
M
is the
\varphi
-stable lattice constructed in (
5.1
).

As in
[
PR94
,
\lx@sectionsign
1.1.3]
, let
\psi
be the
\sigma^{-1}
-semi-linear operator on
K\llbracket X\rrbracket
characterized by
10
10

10

This
\psi
is not the character of the modular form. They are clearly distinguished by the context.

\varphi\circ\psi(f(X))=p^{-1}\cdot\sum_{\zeta\in\mu_{p}}f(\zeta\cdot(1+X)-1)

where
f(X)\in K\llbracket X\rrbracket
.
Then
\psi\circ\varphi=1
.
We define the
\psi=0
subring of
W\llbracket X\rrbracket
by

W\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\psi=0}=\left\{f(X)\in W\llbracket X\rrbracket:\sum_{\zeta\in\mu_{p}}f(\zeta\cdot(1+X)-1)=0\right\}

as in
[
PR94
,
\lx@sectionsign
1.1.6]
and
[
Ben00
,
\lx@sectionsign
4.1.2]
.
It is known that

W\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\psi=0}
is a
W
-module stable under the
\mathrm{Gal}(K_{\infty}/K)
-action (via the cyclotomic character) and
is a free
W\llbracket\mathrm{Gal}(K_{\infty}/K)\rrbracket
-module of rank one generated by
1+X
.
Furthermore, it admits the unique product structure
*
compatible with the
\mathrm{Gal}(K_{\infty}/K)
-module structure such that

(1+X)*(1+X)=(1+X)
. If one interpret
W\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\psi=0}
as the space of
W
-valued measures on
\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}
, then this product structure corresponds to the convolution product. See
[
PR94
,
\lx@sectionsign
3.6.3]
for details.

6.2.
The twist convention

We follow the convention given in
[
PR94
, 2.1.6 Ex.]
.
Let
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(1)
be the
p
-adic representation of
G_{K}
associated with the cyclotomic character.
We put

\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathbb{Q}_{p}(1)^{\otimes i},&i\geq 0,\\
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}(1),\mathbb{Q}_{p})^{\otimes-i},&i<0.\end{array}\right.

Let
K[-i]=\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i))
be the filtered
\varphi
-module associated to
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i)
.
Let
e_{-i}=t^{-i}\otimes\varepsilon^{\otimes i}
(as in
[
BB08
, Thm. 3.1.(2)]
) be the canonical basis of
K
-vector space
K[-i]
. Then we have

\varphi\cdot e_{-i}=p^{-i}\cdot e_{-i}.

The filtration of
K[-i]
is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Fil}^{-i}K[-i]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{D}^{-i}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i))=\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i)),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Fil}^{-i+1}K[-i]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{D}^{-i+1}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i))=0.

Let
V
be a crystalline representation of
G_{K}
and write
V(i)=V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i)
.
Let
D
be a filtered
\varphi
-module associated to a crystalline representation of
G_{K}

and write
D[i]=D\otimes_{K}K[i]
.
Then we have

\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V(i))=\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)[-i]
.
In other words, we have

\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V(i))=\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)\otimes e_{-i}
and

\mathbf{D}^{-j}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V(i))=\mathbf{D}^{i-j}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)\otimes e_{-i}

in terms of the filtration.

Remark 6.1
.

The convention on
e_{-i}
is compatible with
[
Ben00
,
\lx@sectionsign
0.3]
,
[
BB08
, Thm. 3.1.(2)]
, and
[
Ben14
]
, but not with
[
Ber03
, II.5]
and
[
Nak14
, Notation]
.

6.3.
Differential operators

We follow
[
PR94
,
\lx@sectionsign
1.1.5]
.
Let

D=(1+X)\cdot\dfrac{d}{dX}

be the differential operator on
\mathscr{H}_{K}
.
Then we have
D(f)(e^{z}-1)=\dfrac{dh}{dz}(z)
where
h(z)=f(e^{z}-1)
.
For any
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, we have

D(f((1+X)^{\alpha}-1))=\alpha\cdot D(f)((1+X)^{\alpha}-1)
.
Note that
D
is a
W
-linear automorphism of
W\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\psi=0}
; thus,
D^{i}
is defined for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.
If
f\in K\llbracket X\rrbracket
and
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, we define

\Delta_{j}(f)=D^{j}(f)(0)

following Kummer, Coates–Wiles, and Perrin-Riou.

6.4.
Equation
(1-\varphi)G=g

The main references are
[
PR94
,
\lx@sectionsign
2.2]
and
[
Ben00
,
\lx@sectionsign
4.1]
.
We would like to consider equation

(1-\varphi)G=g

in
\mathscr{H}_{K}\otimes_{K}\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)

where
V
is a finite dimensional crystalline representation of
G_{K}
.
The maps

\Delta_{i}:\mathscr{H}_{K}\to K
and
D:\mathscr{H}_{K}\to\mathscr{H}_{K}

naturally induce the maps (with the same notation)

\textstyle{\Delta_{i}:\mathscr{H}_{K}\otimes_{K}\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)\to\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V),}
\textstyle{D:\mathscr{H}_{K}\otimes_{K}\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)\to\mathscr{H}_{K}\otimes_{K}\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V).}

The Frobenius
\varphi
acts on
\mathscr{H}_{K}\otimes_{K}\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)
by
\varphi\otimes\varphi
.
Let
\mathscr{D}(V):=W\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\psi=0}\otimes_{W}\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)
,
which is a
W\llbracket\mathrm{Gal}(K_{\infty}/K)\rrbracket
-module, and

\mathscr{H}(V):=\left\{G\in\mathscr{H}_{K}\otimes_{K}\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V):(1-\varphi)G\in\mathscr{D}(V)\right\}
.
Since
D:W\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\psi=0}\to W\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\psi=0}
is an automorphism,
D
also naturally defines an automorphism of
\mathscr{D}(V)
(with the same notation).

Assumption 6.2
.

Let
h
be any positive integer such that
\mathbf{D}^{-h}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)=\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)
.

Under Assumption
6.2
, we have an exact sequence

(6.1)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{ker}(1-\varphi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{H}(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1-\varphi}
\textstyle{\mathscr{D}(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\Delta}_{[0,h]}}
\textstyle{{\displaystyle\bigoplus_{0\leq j\leq h}\dfrac{\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)}{(1-p^{j}\varphi)\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

with

\mathrm{ker}(1-\varphi)=\sum_{j\geq 0}\left(\mathrm{log}(1+X)\right)^{j}\otimes\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)^{\varphi=p^{-j}}

where
\widetilde{\Delta}_{[0,h]}=\bigoplus_{0\leq j\leq h}\widetilde{\Delta}_{j}
and

\textstyle{\widetilde{\Delta}_{j}:\mathscr{D}(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{j}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
natural
quotient
\textstyle{\dfrac{\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)}{(1-p^{j}\varphi)\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)}.}

See
[
PR94
,
\lx@sectionsign
2.2.7]
for detail.

We now discuss an integral refinement of the above argument following
[
Ben00
,
\lx@sectionsign
4.1.2]
.

Assumption 6.3
.

We further assume that
\mathbf{D}^{0}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)=\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)
, i.e.,
h
can be taken as 0.

Let
T
be a
G_{K}
-stable
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-lattice of
V
and
M
the
\varphi
-stable
W
-lattice of
\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)
associated to
T
as in (
5.1
).
We define

\mathscr{D}(T):=W\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\psi=0}\otimes_{W}M

and

\mathscr{H}(T):=\left\{G\in W\llbracket X\rrbracket\otimes_{W}M:\sum_{\zeta\in\mu_{p}}G(\zeta\cdot(1+X)-1)=p\cdot\varphi G(X)\right\}.

If
G(X)=\alpha(X)\otimes\beta
with
\alpha(X)\in W\llbracket X\rrbracket
and
\beta\in M
, then

\varphi G(X)=\sigma(\alpha)((X+1)^{p}-1)\otimes\varphi(\beta)
.
One has
\mathscr{D}(T)\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}=\mathscr{D}(V)
and
\mathscr{H}(T)\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}=\mathscr{H}(V)
due to
[
Ben00
, Lem. 4.1.3.(i)]
.
Also, if
\mathbf{D}^{0}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)=\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)
(Assumption
6.3
), then
\mathbf{D}^{0}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)=\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)^{\varphi=p^{-j}}=0
for
j>0
due to
[
Ben00
,
\lx@sectionsign
4.1.2]
.
Under Assumption
6.3
,
(
6.1
) becomes the exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)^{\varphi=1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{H}(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1-\varphi}
\textstyle{\mathscr{D}(V)^{\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Following
[
Ben00
, Proof of Lem. 4.1.3]
, the integral refinement of the above sequence also holds

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M^{\varphi=1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{H}(T)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1-\varphi}
\textstyle{\mathscr{D}(T)^{\widetilde{\Delta}_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Solving the equation
(1-\varphi)G=g
is compatible with twists in the following sense.

Lemma 6.4
.

For
j\geq 0
, the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{\mathscr{H}(V(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D^{j}\otimes e_{j}}
\scriptstyle{1-\varphi}
\textstyle{\mathscr{H}(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1-\varphi}
\textstyle{\mathscr{D}(V(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D^{j}\otimes e_{j}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{D}(V).}

Proof.

See
[
Ben00
, Lem. 4.1.3.(ii)]
.
∎

6.5.
Finite layer integral exponential maps with twists

Note that
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{e}=\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f}
under our running assumption (Assumption
1.8
.(ENV)) and
[
BK90
]
.
We recall
[
Ben00
,
\lx@sectionsign
4.2 and Thm. 4.3]
.
Let

\mathrm{exp}_{V(j),K_{n}}:K_{n}\otimes\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V(j))\to\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f}(K_{n},V(j))\subseteq\mathrm{H}^{1}(K_{n},V(j))

be the Bloch–Kato exponential map following
[
BK90
]
with kernel
K_{n}\otimes\mathbf{D}^{0}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V(j))
.

Theorem 6.5

(Benois)
.

For
n\geq 0
, there exist a family of homomorphisms

\textstyle{\Sigma^{\varepsilon}_{T,j,K_{n}}:\mathscr{H}(T)\to\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f}(K_{n},T(j)),}
\textstyle{\Sigma^{\varepsilon}_{V,j,K_{n}}=\Sigma^{\varepsilon}_{T,j,K_{n}}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}:\mathscr{H}(V)\to\mathrm{H}^{1}(K_{n},V(j))}

for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}

satisfying the following relations

(1)

for
G\in\mathscr{H}(T)
and
n\geq 1
,

\mathrm{cor}_{K_{n+1}/K_{n}}\Sigma^{\varepsilon}_{T,j,K_{n+1}}(G)=\Sigma^{\varepsilon}_{T,j,K_{n}}(\varphi(G)).

(2)

for
j\geq 1
and
G_{j}\in\mathscr{H}(V(j))
,

\Sigma^{\varepsilon}_{V,j,K_{n}}(D^{j}G_{j}\otimes e_{j})=(-1)^{j}\cdot(j-1)!\cdot p^{(j-1)n}\cdot\mathrm{exp}_{V(j),K_{n}}(G_{j}(\zeta_{p^{n}}-1)).

Proof.

See
[
Ben00
, Thm. 4.3]
. The proof involves the lattice
M
in (
5.1
) as described in
[
Ben00
,
\lx@sectionsign
4.4.3]
.
∎

Remark 6.6
.

Our
\Sigma^{\varepsilon}_{T,j,K_{n}}
is
\Sigma^{\varepsilon}_{T,j,n}
in
[
Ben00
,
\lx@sectionsign
4.2]
.
In
[
Ben00
, Thm. 4.3]
, there is a lower bound of the index
s
such that Theorem
6.5
holds for
n\geq s
.
Since
s
is the index
n
in Theorem
5.4
following
[
Ben00
,
\lx@sectionsign
4.1.2]
, we can take
s=0
for crystalline representations of
G_{K}
.

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
h
be any integer such that
\mathbf{D}^{-h}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)=\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)
.

(1)

If
G\in M^{\varphi=1}
, then
\Sigma^{\varepsilon}_{T,j,K_{n}}(G)
maps to zero in
\dfrac{\mathrm{H}^{1}(K_{n},T(j))}{\mathrm{H}^{1}(K_{\infty}/K_{n},T(j)^{G_{K_{\infty}}})}
.

(2)

If
j\not\in[0,h]
, then
V(j)^{G_{K_{n}}}=0
; thus,
\mathrm{H}^{1}(K_{\infty}/K_{n},T(j)^{G_{K_{\infty}}})=0
.

Proof.

See
[
Ben00
,
\lx@sectionsign
5.1.6]
. For the second statement, it is essential to observe that zero is not a Hodge–Tate weight of
V(j)
if
j\not\in[0,h]
.
∎

For
j\not\in[0,h]
, the map
\Sigma^{\varepsilon}_{T,j,K_{n}}
induces the homomorphism

\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V,j,K_{n}}:\mathscr{D}(V)^{\widetilde{\Delta}=0}\to\mathrm{H}^{1}(K_{n},V(j))

defined by

(6.2)

\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V,j,K_{n}}(g)=\Sigma^{\varepsilon}_{V,j,K_{n}}(\varphi^{-n}G)

where
(1-\varphi)G=g
as in
\lx@sectionsign
6.4
.
By Theorem
6.5
.(2), we have

(6.3)

\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V,j,K_{n}}(g)=(-1)^{j}\cdot(j-1)!\cdot\mathrm{exp}_{V(j),K_{n}}(\Xi^{\varepsilon}_{V,j,K_{n}}(g))

for
j\geq 1

where

\Xi^{\varepsilon}_{V,j,K_{n}}(g)=p^{-n}\cdot\varphi^{-n}G_{j}(\zeta_{p^{n}}-1)

and
G_{j}\in\mathscr{H}(V(j))
is a solution to the equation
(1-\varphi)G_{j}=(D^{-j}\otimes e_{-j})(g)
.
By Lemma
6.4
, we have

(6.4)

(D^{-j}\otimes e_{-j})G=G_{j}.

See
[
Ben00
,
\lx@sectionsign
5.2.3]
for detail.
Thus, one has

\Xi^{\varepsilon}_{V,j,K_{n}}=\Xi^{\varepsilon}_{V(j),0,K_{n}}\circ(D^{-j}\otimes e_{-j})

as in
[
BB08
,
\lx@sectionsign
3.1]
.
We also have

\mathrm{cor}_{K_{n+1}/K_{n}}\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V,j,K_{n+1}}(g)=\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V,j,K_{n}}(g)

by Theorem
6.5
.(1).

6.6.
Perrin-Riou’s big exponential map

We carefully state the combination of the theorems of Benois and Berger on a generalization and refinement of Perrin-Riou’s big exponential map for crystalline representations over unramified base fields.

Let

\Xi^{\varepsilon}_{V(j),0,K_{n}}:\mathscr{D}(V(j))^{\widetilde{\Delta}=0}\to K_{n}\otimes\dfrac{\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V(j))}{\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V(j))^{\varphi=1}}

be the map defined by

g\mapsto p^{-n}\cdot\varphi^{-n}G_{j}(\zeta_{p^{n}}-1)

where
(1-\varphi)G_{j}(X)=(D^{-j}\otimes e_{-j})(g)(X)
and
G_{j}(X)\in\mathscr{H}(V(j))
.
Write
\Gamma=\mathrm{Gal}(K_{\infty}/K)\simeq\Gamma^{1}\times\Delta
with
\Delta\simeq\mathbb{F}^{\times}_{p}
, and it justifies the notation
\mathscr{H}_{K}(\Gamma)=\mathscr{H}_{K}[\Delta]
by sending a topological generator of
\Gamma^{1}
to
1+X
.
Write

{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(K,T(j))=\varprojlim_{n}\mathrm{H}^{1}(K_{n},T(j))}

where the transition maps are the corestriction maps.

Theorem 6.8

(Benois–Berger–Perrin-Riou)
.

Let
K
be a finite unramified extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
V
a crystalline representation of
G_{K}
.
For any integers
h
and
j
such that

\mathbf{D}^{-h}_{\mathrm{dR}}(V)=\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{dR}}(V)
and

h+j\geq 1
,
there exists a unique
\mathscr{H}_{K}(\Gamma)
-homomorphism

\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V(j),h+j}:\mathscr{D}(V(j))^{\widetilde{\Delta}=0}\to\mathscr{H}_{K}(\Gamma)\otimes_{\Lambda}\dfrac{\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(K,T(j))}{T(j)^{G_{K_{\infty}}}}

satisfying the following properties:

(1)

For any
n\geq 0
, the diagram

(6.5)

\begin{split}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 71.89073pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-29.06058pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise commutes.

(2)

For any integer
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V(j),h+j}
can be defined by the formula

(6.6)

\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V(j+1),h+j+1}=-\mathrm{Tw}^{\varepsilon}_{V(j),1}\circ\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V(j),h+j}\circ(D\otimes e_{1})

where
\mathrm{Tw}^{\varepsilon}_{V(j),1}
sends
x\in\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(K,T(j))
to
x\otimes\varepsilon\in\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(K,T(j+1))
.

(3)

One has

(6.7)

\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V(j),h+j+1}=\ell_{h+j}\cdot\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V(j),h+j}.

where
\ell_{h+j}:=(h+j)-\dfrac{\mathrm{log}(\gamma_{1})}{\mathrm{log}(\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}(\gamma_{1}))}
where
\gamma_{1}
is any topological generator of
\Gamma^{1}
.
Especially, by using (
6.7
),
\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V,h}
can be defined for any integer
h\in\mathbb{Z}
if we invert
\ell_{i}
in
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

See

[
PR94
,
\lx@sectionsign
3.2.3 Thm.]
,

[
BB08
, Thm. 3.1]
, and
[
Ben14
, Thm. 1.3.2]
. In (
6.5
),
\mathrm{exp}_{V(j),K_{n}}
is well-defined due to Lemma
6.7
.(1).
Note that

\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V(j),h+j}
is often denoted by
\mathrm{Exp}^{\varepsilon}_{V(j),h+j}
in the references.
∎

Remark 6.9
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{D}^{0}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)=\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)
.
Then we have
\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V(1))=\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V(1))
.
Applying Theorem
6.8
to
V(1)
with
h=1
and
j=0
and using (
6.6
), we obtain the map

\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V,0}:\mathscr{D}(V)^{\widetilde{\Delta}=0}\to\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\otimes_{\Lambda}\dfrac{\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(K,T(j))}{T(j)^{G_{K_{\infty}}}}

defined by

\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V,0}=-\mathrm{Tw}^{\varepsilon}_{V(1),-1}\circ\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V(1),1}\circ(D^{-1}\otimes e_{-1})

without inverting any element in
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
since
D
is an automorphism as in
\lx@sectionsign
6.3
.

We keep assumption
\mathbf{D}^{0}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)=\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)
in Remark
6.9
.
Following
[
Ben00
, Thm. 5.2.4]
and
[
BB08
, Page 634]
, we twist diagram (
6.5
) as follows.
For
j>0
(with consideration of Lemma
6.7
.(2)), we have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathscr{D}(V)^{\widetilde{\Delta}=0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Tw}^{\varepsilon}_{V,j}\circ\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V,0}}
\scriptstyle{\Xi^{\varepsilon}_{V,j,K_{n}}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\otimes_{\Lambda}\dfrac{\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(K,T(j))}{T(j)^{G_{K_{\infty}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{pr}_{T(j),K_{n}}}
\textstyle{K_{n}\otimes\dfrac{\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V(j))}{\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V(j))^{\varphi=1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-1)^{j}\cdot(j-1)!\cdot\mathrm{exp}_{V(j),K_{n}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}(K_{n},V(j))}

and we have

(6.8)

\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V,j,K_{n}}=\mathrm{pr}_{T(j),K_{n}}\circ\mathrm{Tw}^{\varepsilon}_{V,j}\circ\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V,0}

as in
[
Ben00
, Cor. 5.2.5]
.

3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{K_{n}\otimes\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}(1))}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{split}

where the maps in the rightmost are given by

\textstyle{K_{n}\otimes\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}(1))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{e_{-1}\mapsto 1}
\textstyle{K_{n}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Tr}_{K_{n}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Q}_{p}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{2}(K_{n},\mathbb{Q}_{p}(1)).}

See
[
BK90
, Prop. 3.8]
and
[
Kat93
, Chap. II, Thm. 1.4.1.(4)]
for details.
Following
[
PR94
,
\lx@sectionsign
3.6.1]
, we consider the Iwasawa-theoretic variation of (
6.9
).
We first restrict
[-,-]_{V,K_{n}}
to

[-,-]_{T,K_{n}}:\mathrm{H}^{1}(K_{n},T)\times\mathrm{H}^{1}(K_{n},T^{*}(1))\to\mathbb{Z}_{p}

where
T^{*}(1)=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(T,\mathbb{Z}_{p})(1)
.
For
x=(x_{n})\in\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(K,T)
and
y=(y_{n})\in\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(K,T^{*}(1))
,
we define the
Perrin-Riou pairing

\langle-,-\rangle_{T,K}:\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(K,T)\times\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(K,T^{*}(1))\to\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}w}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket\mathrm{Gal}(K_{\infty}/K)\rrbracket

by

\langle x,y\rangle_{T,K}:=\varprojlim_{n}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Gal}(K_{n}/K)}[(x_{n})^{\sigma^{-1}},y_{n}]_{T,K_{n}}\cdot\sigma\in\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}w}
.
It satisfies

\langle\lambda\cdot x,y\rangle_{T,K}=\lambda\cdot\langle x,y\rangle_{T,K}=\langle x,\iota(\lambda)\cdot y\rangle_{T,K}

where
\lambda\in\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}w}
and
\iota:\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}w}\to\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}w}
sends
\gamma\mapsto\gamma^{-1}
for
\gamma\in\Gamma
.
We extend the pairing linearly

\mathscr{H}_{K}(\Gamma)\otimes_{\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}w}}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(K,T)\times\mathscr{H}_{K}(\Gamma)\otimes_{\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}w}}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(K,T^{*}(1))\to\mathscr{H}_{K}(\Gamma).

Since
p
is inverted in
\mathscr{H}_{K}(\Gamma)
, the torsion in
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathcal{I}w}(K,T)
is ignored.
Using the product structure
*
on
W\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\psi=0}
described in
\lx@sectionsign
6.1
, we
extend
[-,-]_{\mathbf{D}(V)}=[-,-]_{\mathbf{D}(V),K}
to a bilinear form of
\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}w}
-modules

[-,-]_{\mathscr{D}(V)}:\mathscr{D}(V)\times\mathscr{D}(V^{*}(1))\to K\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}w}

such that

[g_{1}\otimes d_{1},g_{2}\otimes d_{2}]_{\mathscr{D}(V)}=[d_{1},d_{2}]_{\mathbf{D}(V)}\cdot g_{1}*g_{2}\in K\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{I}w}

for

g_{1},g_{2}\in W\llbracket X\rrbracket^{\psi=0}
,
d_{1}\in\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V)
, and
d_{2}\in\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V^{*}(1))
.

The following conjecture (e.g.
[
PR94
,
\lx@sectionsign
3.6.4]
,
[
PR99
, Thm. 4.2.3]
) is known as the
explicit reciprocity law
conjecture, which is now a theorem.

Conjecture 6.10

(
\mathrm{R\acute{e}c}(V)
)
.

Let
V
be a crystalline representation of
G_{K}
. For all
x\in\mathscr{D}(V)^{\widetilde{\Delta}=0}
,
y\in\mathscr{D}(V^{*}(1))^{\widetilde{\Delta}=0}
,
we have

\left\langle\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V,h}(x),\Omega^{\varepsilon^{-1}}_{V^{*}(1),1-h}(y)\right\rangle_{V}\cdot(1+X)=(-1)^{h-1}\cdot\mathrm{Tr}_{K/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\left(\left[x,y\right]_{\mathscr{D}(V)}\right)

for any integer
h
.
In other words, the inverse of
\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V,h}
is the adjoint of
\Omega^{\varepsilon^{-1}}_{V^{*}(1),1-h}
up to sign.

Theorem 6.11

(Benois, Berger, Colmez, Kato–Kurihara–Tsuji)
.

Conjecture
6.10
holds.

Remarks on proof.

Conjecture
6.10
was proved by Benois
[
Ben00
]
with
[
Col99
]
(Theorem
5.3
) and Colmez
[
Col98
]
independently. There are also different proofs by Berger
[
Ber03
]
and Kato–Kurihara–Tsuji
[
KKT
]
.
∎

Remark 6.12
.

(1)

Although the image of
\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V,h}
and
\Omega^{\varepsilon^{-1}}_{V^{*}(1),1-h}

lie in the quotients of Iwasawa cohomologies by their torsions, the Perrin-Riou pairing is still well-defined. See
[
PR94
, 3.6.2.Lem.]
.

(2)

If one constructs
\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V,h}
or
\Omega^{\varepsilon^{-1}}_{V^{*}(1),1-h}
from Bloch–Kato exponentials, then one can also (equivalently) construct the other from Bloch–Kato dual exponentials.
In our case, we construct
\Omega^{\varepsilon^{-1}}_{V_{f}(k)^{*}(1),0}=\Omega^{\varepsilon^{-1}}_{V_{\overline{f}},0}

from Bloch–Kato exponential maps.
In
[
Lei11
,
\lx@sectionsign
3.2 and
\lx@sectionsign
3.3.2]
and
[
LLZ10
,
\lx@sectionsign
3.5.1]
, Lei–Loeffler–Zerbes construct
\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V_{f}(1),1}
from Bloch–Kato exponential maps.
This discrepancy affects the interpolation formula of
p
-adic
L
-functions very slightly. See
\lx@sectionsign
7.7
for details.

Proposition 6.13
.

(1)

For
\tau\in\mathrm{Gal}(K_{\infty}/K)
,

\Omega^{\tau\varepsilon}_{V,h}=\tau\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V,h}=\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{V,h}\circ\tau

(2)

\mathrm{Tw}^{\varepsilon}_{-j,V^{*}(1)}=(-1)^{j}\cdot\mathrm{Tw}^{\varepsilon^{-1}}_{-j,V^{*}(1)}

Proof.

See
[
PR94
, 3.2.4.Pre. rem. (v) and 3.6.5.Lem.]

∎

7.
Mazur–Tate elements II: étale construction

Refining Perrin-Riou’s local Iwasawa theory reviewed in
\lx@sectionsign
6
, we develop an
equivariant
local Iwasawa theory and apply it to modular forms
in an
explicit
manner.
As a by-product, we construct the
equivariant

p
-adic
L
-functions of modular forms directly from Kato’s Euler systems and the Mazur–Tate elements of modular forms over general cyclotomic extensions of
\mathbb{Q}
.
In other words, we expand and refine
[
Kat04
, Line 1, Page 270]
.
More precisely, we give explicit proofs of the corrected versions of
[
Kat04
, Thm. 16.4 and Thm. 16.6]
on the construction of
p
-adic
L
-functions of modular forms at good primes (i.e. the crystalline case) from Kato’s Euler systems and generalize them equivariantly. The key input is the explicit construction of the families of equivariant local points for modular forms following the idea of Perrin-Riou
[
PR94
,
\lx@sectionsign
2.3]
and Ota
[
Ota23
]
.
It can be regarded as a finite layer refinement of the explicit reciprocity law for modular forms and generalizes the constructions given in
[
Kur02
,
Ots09
,
Ota18
,
Ota23
,
Kat21
]
.

7.1.
Non-triviality of equivariant local points

We regard
V_{\overline{f}}
as a crystalline representation of
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
via restriction.
Then the corresponding filtered
\varphi
-module
\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{\overline{f}})
has filtration given by

\mathbf{D}^{i}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{\overline{f}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{\overline{f}})&(i\leq 0)\\
S(\overline{f})\otimes_{F}F_{\pi}&(1\leq i\leq k-1)\\
0&(k\leq i)\end{array}\right.

as in
[
Kat04
, (11.3.4)]
.

Definition 7.1
.

Following (
5.1
), we define the
\varphi
-stable
\mathcal{O}_{\pi}
-lattice of
\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{\overline{f}})
by

M_{\overline{f}}:=\left(\mathbf{D}_{S}(T_{\overline{f}})\widehat{\otimes}_{S}\widehat{S}^{\mathrm{PD}}\right)^{\Gamma}\subseteq\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{\overline{f}})

where
\Gamma=\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Remark 7.2
.

When
2\leq k\leq p-1
, one may use the lattice arising from Fontaine–Laffaille modules instead of
M_{\overline{f}}
.
See

[
BK90
, Thm. 4.1.(iii)]
,

[
PR94
,
\lx@sectionsign
2.1.5]
, and

[
Kat04
,
\lx@sectionsign
14.17]
for details.

Let
\overline{\alpha}\in\mathbb{C}_{p}
be a root of
X^{2}-a_{p}(\overline{f})X+\overline{\psi}(p)p^{k-1}

such that
\mathrm{ord}_{p}(\overline{\alpha})<k-1
and
\overline{\beta}
the other root.
Both
\overline{\alpha}
and
\overline{\beta}
cannot be a root of unity due to their complex absolute values, and all
\alpha
,
\beta
,
\overline{\alpha}
, and
\overline{\beta}
lie in
\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]
.

Proposition 7.3
.

(1)

Suppose that
\overline{f}
is non-ordinary at
p
.
Then the map

\left(1-\varphi\right):(\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{m}]\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}M_{\overline{f}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]\to(\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{m}]\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}M_{\overline{f}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]

is surjective.

(2)

Suppose that
\overline{f}
is ordinary at
p
with unit Hecke eigenvalue
\overline{\alpha}
at
p
.
Then the map

\left(1-\varphi\right):\dfrac{1}{1-\overline{\alpha}^{\phi(m)}}\cdot(\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{m}]\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}M_{\overline{f}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]\to(\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{m}]\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}M_{\overline{f}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]

is surjective.

Proof.

We follow
[
Ota23
]
.

(1)

Under the non-ordinary assumption,

\overline{\alpha}^{n}\not\equiv 1\pmod{\pi}
and
\overline{\beta}^{n}\not\equiv 1\pmod{\pi}
for any integer
n\geq 1
.
Let
g\in(\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{m}]\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes M_{\overline{f}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]
.
Since the
\varphi
-eigenvalues of
M_{\overline{f}}
are non-units,
G=\sum_{n\geq 0}\varphi^{n}(x)
converges and it satisfies
(1-\varphi)G=g
.

(2)

Let
g\in(\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{m}]\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}M_{\overline{f}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]
.
Write
g=a\cdot g_{\overline{\alpha}}+b\cdot g_{\overline{\beta}}

where
a,b\in\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{m}]\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and

g_{\overline{\alpha}},g_{\overline{\beta}}\in M_{\overline{f}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]

such that

\varphi g_{\overline{\alpha}}=\overline{\alpha}\cdot g_{\overline{\alpha}}
and
\varphi g_{\overline{\beta}}=\overline{\beta}\cdot g_{\overline{\beta}}
.
We write

\textstyle{(1-\overline{\alpha}^{\phi(m)})\cdot G_{\overline{\alpha}}=\sum_{0\leq i\leq\phi(m)-1}\varphi^{i}(a\cdot g_{\overline{\alpha}}),}
\textstyle{G_{\overline{\beta}}=\sum_{n\geq 0}\varphi^{n}(b\cdot g_{\overline{\beta}})}

and then
G_{\overline{\alpha}}
lies only in
\dfrac{1}{1-\overline{\alpha}^{\phi(m)}}\cdot(\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{m}]\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\otimes M_{\overline{f}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]
.
Since
\sigma^{\phi(m)}=1
on
\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{m}]\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, we have
(1-\varphi)(G_{\overline{\alpha}}+G_{\overline{\beta}})=g
.
Thus, we have the surjectivity of

\left(1-\varphi\right):\dfrac{1}{1-\overline{\alpha}^{\phi(m)}}\cdot(\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{m}]\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}M_{\overline{f}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]\to(\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{m}]\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}M_{\overline{f}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha].

∎

Let

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(T_{\overline{f}})

\displaystyle=\left\{G\in\left(\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{m}]\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)\llbracket X\rrbracket\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}M_{\overline{f}}:\sum_{\zeta\in\mu_{p}}G(\zeta(1+X)-1)=p\cdot\varphi G(X)\right\}

\displaystyle=\left(\left(\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{m}]\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)\llbracket X\rrbracket\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}M_{\overline{f}}\right)^{\psi=1},

and

\mathscr{D}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(T_{\overline{f}})=\left(\left(\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{m}]\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)\llbracket X\rrbracket\right)^{\psi=0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}M_{\overline{f}}
.

In order to make everything
\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})/\mathbb{Q})
-equivariant, we fix a primitive
m
-th root of unity
\zeta_{m}\in\mu_{m}
.

Choice 7.4
.

Let
\varepsilon_{m}=\left(\zeta_{mp^{n}}\right)_{n\geq 0}
be the choice of the compatible system of
mp^{n}
-th roots of unity compatible with
\varepsilon
chosen in Choice
5.1
.

Let

\mathrm{exp}_{V_{\overline{f}}(r),\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})}:\left(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}\right)\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\left(\dfrac{\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{\overline{f}}(r))}{\mathrm{Fil}^{0}\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{\overline{f}}(r))}\right)\to\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{\overline{f}}(r))

be the direct sum of the local Bloch–Kato exponential maps and then it is
\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})/\mathbb{Q})
-equivariant.
The equivariant property allows us to define a family of homomorphism

\Sigma^{\varepsilon_{m}}_{T_{\overline{f}},r,\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})}:\mathscr{H}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(T_{\overline{f}})\to\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{\overline{f}}(r))

for
r\in\mathbb{Z}
by

\Sigma^{\varepsilon_{m}}_{T_{\overline{f}},r,\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})}=\bigoplus_{v\mid p}\Sigma^{\varepsilon_{m}}_{T_{\overline{f}},r,\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})_{v}}
where
v
runs over all primes of
\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})
lying above
p
.
Note that

\bigoplus_{v\mid p}\Sigma^{\varepsilon_{m}}_{T_{\overline{f}},r,\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})_{v}}=\bigoplus_{v\mid p}\Sigma^{\varepsilon}_{T_{\overline{f}},r,\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})_{v}}

as
\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})/\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}))
-homomorphisms, but the former one also remembers the action of
\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})/\mathbb{Q})
under Choice
7.4
. This extension is natural due to the equivariant property of
\mathrm{exp}_{V_{\overline{f}}(r),\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}
.
Thus, Theorem
6.5
extends equivariantly in the natural manner.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
\gamma_{m}\in\Gamma
such that
\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}(\gamma_{m})=m\in\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{p}
.

(1)

Suppose that
\overline{f}
is non-ordinary at
p
.
For each
\eta\in M_{\overline{f}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]
, there exists
a unique

G_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\eta}\in\mathscr{H}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(T_{\overline{f}})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]

such that

(1-\varphi)G_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\eta}=\dfrac{1}{m}\cdot\left(\zeta_{m}(1+X)^{1/m}\otimes\eta\right)
.

(2)

Suppose that
\overline{f}
is ordinary at
p
.
For each
\eta\in M_{\overline{f}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]
, there exists
a unique

G_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\eta}\in\dfrac{1}{1-\overline{\alpha}^{\phi(m)}}\cdot\mathscr{H}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}(T_{\overline{f}})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]

such that

(1-\varphi)G_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\eta}=\dfrac{1}{m}\cdot\left(\zeta_{m}(1+X)^{1/m}\otimes\eta\right)
.

Proof.

Straightforward from Proposition
7.3
.
∎

Remark 7.6
.

(1)

In the non-ordinary case, we have
(1-\overline{\alpha}^{\phi(m)})\in\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]^{\times}
.

(2)

The choice of
\zeta_{m}
(Choice
7.4
) is explicitly used in Lemma
7.5
.
We give an explicit solution of
(1-\varphi)G=g
when
g=m^{r-1}\cdot\zeta_{m}\cdot(1+X)^{1/m}\otimes\eta_{\overline{\alpha}}\otimes e_{-r}

in order to obtain the precise interpolation formula in
\lx@sectionsign
7.4
.

By twisting the bottom row in (
6.9
) with the duality
\mathrm{Hom}_{F_{\pi}}(V_{f}(k-r),F_{\pi})(1)\simeq V_{\overline{f}}(r)
, we have the natural paring

[-,-]:\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f})\times\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{\overline{f}})\to\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(F_{\pi}(1-k))\simeq F_{\pi}

where the last isomorphism sends
e_{k-1}\mapsto 1
as in (
6.9
).

Proposition 7.7
.

We recall the following assumption:

Assumption
1.8
.(“non-CM”). If
\overline{f}
is ordinary at
p
, the restriction of
\overline{\rho}_{\overline{f}}
to
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
is not a direct sum of two characters.

Under this assumption, there exist elements

\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\alpha}},\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\beta}}\in\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{\overline{f}})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]

such that

\textstyle{\varphi\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\alpha}}=\overline{\alpha}\cdot\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\alpha}},}
\textstyle{[\omega_{f},\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\alpha}}]\neq 0,}
\textstyle{\varphi\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\beta}}=\overline{\beta}\cdot\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\beta}},}
\textstyle{[\omega_{f},\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\beta}}]\neq 0}

where
\omega_{f}\in S(f)\subseteq\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f})
arises from the normalized eigenform
f
.

Proof.

In the non-ordinary case, the existence of such
\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\alpha}}
and
\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\beta}}
follows from
[
Kat04
, Thm. 16.6.(1)]
.
In the ordinary case, the existence of
\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\alpha}}
also follows from
[
Kat04
, Thm. 16.6.(1)]
; however, the existence of
\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\beta}}
in the ordinary case follows from
[
Col04
, Rem. 4.12]
under Assumption
1.8
.(“non-CM”) since it is of critical slope.
∎

7.2.
Uniform integrality of equivariant local points

For an
\mathcal{O}_{\pi}
-module
B
, we put

B^{*}:=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}(B,\mathcal{O}_{\pi})
.
We recall the cup product pairing

[-,-]_{V_{f}(k-r),\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})}:\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(k-r))\times\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{\overline{f}}(r))\to F_{\pi},

and consider its
optimally integral
restriction

[-,-]_{V_{f}(k-r),\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})}:\mathrm{H}^{1}_{/f}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(k-r))\times\mathrm{H}^{1}_{/f}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(k-r))^{*}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\pi}

where
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{/f}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(k-r))^{*}\subseteq\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{\overline{f}}(r))
is characterized the surjectivity of the pairing.

Lemma 7.8
.

Under Assumption
1.8
.(“non-CM”),

\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},W_{\overline{f}}(r))=0

for any integer
m
with
(m,p)=1
and any
1\leq r\leq k-1
.

Proof.

It suffices to show
\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},W_{\overline{f},-i}(r)[\pi])=0
.
By
[
Gro90
,
CV92
]
, Assumption
1.8
.(“non-CM”) implies that
W_{\overline{f}}(r)[\pi]
is wildly ramified at
p
. Thus, the restriction of
W_{\overline{f}}(r)[\pi]
to
G_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}
is still wildly ramified at all primes above
p
. Thus, the local Galois representation does not split, so the conclusion follows.
∎

Proposition 7.9
.

We keep Assumption
1.8
.(“non-CM”), and we also assume that
\overline{\alpha}\neq\zeta\cdot p^{\frac{k-1}{2}}
when
r=\frac{k-1}{2}
is an integer where
\zeta
is a root of unity (Assumption
1.8
.(ENV)).
By choosing
\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\alpha}}
and
\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\beta}}
as in Proposition
7.7
, we are able to make the elements

\Sigma^{\varepsilon_{m}}_{T_{\overline{f}},r,\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})}(G^{\sigma^{-n}}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\alpha}}}),\Sigma^{\varepsilon_{m}}_{T_{\overline{f}},r,\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})}(G^{\sigma^{-n}}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\beta}}})\in\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{\overline{f}}(r))\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]

to lie in

\mathrm{H}^{1}_{/f}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(k-r))^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]

via the integral cup product pairing
where

1\leq r\leq k-1
, and
\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})
runs over all
n\geq 0
and
m\geq 1
with
(m,p)=1
.

Proof.

Due to Proposition
7.7
, we can take

\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\alpha}}
and

\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\beta}}
satisfying

\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\alpha}},\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\beta}}\in M_{\overline{f}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]
by scaling.
By Theorem
6.5
, Lemma
7.5

and the construction of
\Sigma^{\varepsilon_{m}}_{T_{\overline{f}},r,\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})}
in
[
Ben00
,
\lx@sectionsign
4.2]
, it is immediate to have

\Sigma^{\varepsilon_{m}}_{T_{\overline{f}},r,\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})}(G^{\sigma^{-n}}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\bullet})\in\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{\overline{f}}(r))\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]

for all
\bullet\in\{\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\alpha}},\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\beta}}\}
unless
\bullet=\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\alpha}}
and
\overline{f}
is ordinary.
Even when
\bullet=\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\alpha}}
and
\overline{f}
is ordinary,

\Sigma^{\varepsilon_{m}}_{T_{\overline{f}},r,\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})}(G^{\sigma^{-n}}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\alpha}}})
still lies in
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{\overline{f}}(r))\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]

due to the explicit computation in
\lx@sectionsign
7.4
below.
More specifically, it essentially follows from the fact that the eigenvalue of
(1-\varphi)^{-1}
on
\eta^{\prime}_{\overline{\alpha}}\otimes e_{-r}
is
\dfrac{1}{1-\overline{\alpha}^{\phi(m)}\cdot p^{-r}}=\dfrac{p^{r}}{p^{r}-\overline{\alpha}^{\phi(m)}}
and it is integral since
1\leq r\leq k-1
.
Note that
p^{r}-\overline{\alpha}^{\phi(m)}\neq 0
under our assumption with Weil number considerations.
The local duality argument in proof of
[
Kat04
, Lem. 14.18]
with Lemma
7.8
implies

\Sigma^{\varepsilon_{m}}_{T_{\overline{f}},r,\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})}(G^{\sigma^{-n}}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\bullet})\in\mathrm{H}^{1}_{/f}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(k-r))^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]

as
\left[-,\Sigma^{\varepsilon_{m}}_{T_{\overline{f}},r,\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})}(G^{\sigma^{-n}}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\bullet})\right]_{V_{f}(k-r),\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})}
.∎

Remark 7.10
.

The upshot of Proposition
7.9
is that the integrality of
\Sigma^{\varepsilon_{m}}_{T_{\overline{f}},r,\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})}(G^{\sigma^{-n}}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\eta})

in
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{/f}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(k-r))^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]

depends only on the choice of
\eta\in\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{\overline{f}})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\alpha]
.
Proposition
7.9
uses the theory of Wach modules in a strong way.

7.3.
Crystalline normalization of equivariant local points

Let

\mathrm{per}^{\pm}_{f}:\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp^{n}})\otimes S(f)\to\mathbb{C}\otimes_{F}V_{F}(f)\to\mathbb{C}\otimes_{F}V^{\pm}_{F}(f)
be the projection of Kato’s period integrals to the
\pm
-component, respectively.

We extend the comparison (
3.2
) further including the crystalline comparison isomorphism due to Tsuji, Faltings, Nizioł and others.

(7.1)

The naïve integral refinement of (
7.1
) (with certain unclear parts) can be written as follows

(7.2)

where
\omega_{f}\in S(f)\otimes F_{\pi}=\mathbf{D}^{k-r}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{F_{\pi}}(f))
is the class coming from the

f
and the sign convention follows that of (

).
In (

), the upper part is normalized by

and the lower part is normalized by

.

Theorem
6.5
(when

) and the local duality argument in
[

, Lem. 14.18]
yield an integral refinement of (
6.9
)

(7.3)

{\scriptsize\begin{split}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 1.99306pt\hbox{\kern
