Thus, it suffices to prove the converse.
Suppose that

\overline{D_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}\omega^{i}\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp}),r}(\overline{f})\right)}
does not vanish for some
m\in\mathcal{N}
.
Then

\overline{D_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,i,k-r)}
does not vanish since

D_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}\omega^{i}\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp}),r}(\overline{f})\right)=\mathrm{Col}^{(i)}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),r}\circ\mathrm{loc}^{s}_{p}\left(D_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,i,k-r)\right).

By the above equivalence,

D_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,i,k-r)
does not vanish modulo
\mathfrak{a}_{m}
.
We now suppose that

D_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}\omega^{i}\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp}),r}(\overline{f})\right)
vanishes modulo
\mathfrak{a}_{m}
for every
m\in\mathcal{N}
.
As mentioned above, the non-triviality properties of

\textstyle{\left\{\overline{D_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,i,k-r)}\right\}_{m}\textrm{ and }\left\{D_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,i,k-r)\pmod{\mathfrak{a}_{m}}\right\}_{m}}
D_
Q
(
ζ
_m)
z
_
Q
(
ζ
_m),
δ
_f, cris(f, i, k-r)

are equivalent.
By the equivalence stated before Theorem
9.5
,
\boldsymbol{\kappa}^{(1)}
becomes trivial under the assumption.
Then
\overline{D_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}\omega^{i}\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp}),r}(\overline{f})\right)}
should vanish for every
m\in\mathcal{N}
, but it is a contradiction.
Thus, the following statements are also equivalent.

•

\overline{D_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}\omega^{i}\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp}),r}(\overline{f})\right)}
does not vanish for some
m\in\mathcal{N}
.

•

D_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})}\omega^{i}\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp}),r}(\overline{f})\right)
does not vanish modulo
\mathfrak{a}_{m}
for some
m\in\mathcal{N}
.

Thus, we obtain the equivalence we need.

When
i=0
, it is not difficult to observe that

\displaystyle\omega^{0}\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp}),r}(\overline{f})\right)

\displaystyle=\left(1-a_{p}(\overline{f})\cdot p^{-r}\cdot\mathrm{Frob}^{-1}_{p}+\overline{\psi}(p)\cdot p^{k-1-2r}\cdot\mathrm{Frob}^{-2}_{p}\right)\cdot\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),r}(\overline{f}),

\displaystyle\omega^{0}\left(\mathrm{Col}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp}),r}\left(-\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\left(1-a_{p}(\overline{f})\cdot p^{-r}\cdot\mathrm{Frob}^{-1}_{p}+\overline{\psi}(p)\cdot p^{k-1-2r}\cdot\mathrm{Frob}^{-2}_{p}\right)\cdot\mathrm{Col}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),r}\left(-\right)

since Kato’s Euler systems have no contribution to the
p
-power direction in the norm compatibility.
Write
E_{p}(\overline{f},r)=1-a_{p}(\overline{f})\cdot p^{-r}\cdot\mathrm{Frob}^{-1}_{p}+\overline{\psi}(p)\cdot p^{k-1-2r}\cdot\mathrm{Frob}^{-2}_{p}
and note that
\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,0,k-r)=\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,k-r)
.
Then we have

\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),T_{f}(k-r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{loc}^{s}_{p}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,k-r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}_{/f}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f,i}(k-r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Col}^{(0)}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),r}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{loc}^{s}_{p}\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,k-r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E_{p}(\overline{f},r)\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\pi}[\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})/\mathbb{Q})]}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Col}^{(0)}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{mp}),r}\mathrm{loc}^{s}_{p}\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,k-r)}

and

\mathrm{Col}^{(0)}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),r}\mathrm{loc}_{p}\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,k-r)=E_{p}(\overline{f},r)\cdot\mathrm{Col}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),r}\mathrm{loc}_{p}\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}),\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,k-r)
.
Since the Frobenius acts trivially on Kurihara numbers, we observe that
the
p
-th Euler factor
1-a_{p}(\overline{f})\cdot p^{-r}+\overline{\psi}(p)\cdot p^{k-1-2r}
appears, but it cancels out if it is non-zero.
Thanks to Assumption
1.8
.(ENV), we are safe.
Thus, the Kurihara number for the
i=0
case naturally appears from this computation.

10.
Towards Conjecture A of Coates–Sujatha

We prove Theorem
10.1
in
\lx@sectionsign
10.1
and Corollary
10.2
in
\lx@sectionsign
10.2
and
\lx@sectionsign
10.3
.

Theorem 10.1

(Conjecture A)
.

Let
p
be an odd prime.
Keep Assumption
1.8
as well as Conjecture
1.10
(e.g.
2\leq k\leq p-1
or
\overline{\rho}
is
p
-ordinary and
p
-distinguished).
If

\sum_{a\in(\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\overline{\lambda^{\pm,\mathrm{opt}}(\overline{f},z^{r-1},a(1+bp),p^{n})}\cdot\overline{\omega^{i}(a)}\neq 0

in
\mathbb{F}_{\pi}
for some
b\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{n-1}\mathbb{Z}
with
n\geq 1
, then
then we obtain the
\mu
-part of the main conjecture and Conjecture A of Coates–Sujatha

\mu\left(\dfrac{\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f,i}(k-r))}{\Lambda^{(i)}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\mathbf{z}_{\mathbb{Q},\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,i,k-r)}\right)=\mu\left(\mathrm{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f,i}(k-r))\right)=0.

The verification of Conjecture A is essential to complete the numerical verification of the Iwasawa main conjecture for families of modular forms of arbitrary level via congruences (Corollary
1.6
).

The same numerical criterion also implies the
\mu=0
conjecture for
p
-adic
L
-functions of elliptic curves with good ordinary reduction (Greenberg’s conjecture
[
Gre99
, Conj. 1.11]
) and for
\pm
-
p
-adic
L
-functions of elliptic curves with good supersingular reduction (Pollack’s conjecture
[
Pol03
, Conj. 6.3]
).

Corollary 10.2
.

Assume that
\rho_{f}
has large image.
Let
E
be an (optimal) elliptic curve over
\mathbb{Q}
corresponding to
f
, i.e.
F=\mathbb{Q}
,
\psi=\mathbf{1}
,
f=\overline{f}
,
k=2
, and
r=1
.
If

\sum_{a\in(\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\overline{\lambda^{\pm,\mathrm{opt}}(f,1,a(1+bp),p^{n})}\neq 0

in
\mathbb{F}_{p}
for some
b\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{n-1}\mathbb{Z}
with
n\geq 1
,
then the following statements hold.

(1)

When
E
is good ordinary at
p
and
a_{p}(E)\not\equiv 1\pmod{p}
, we obtain

\mu\left(\Lambda_{\mathrm{Iw}}/L_{p}(E)\right)=\mu\left(\mathrm{Sel}(\mathbb{Q}_{\infty},E[p^{\infty}])^{\vee}\right)=0.

(2)

When
E
is good supersingular at
p
with
a_{p}(E)=0
, we obtain

\begin{array}[]{ll}\mu\left(\Lambda_{\mathrm{Iw}}/L^{-}_{p}(E)\right)=\mu\left(\mathrm{Sel}^{+}(\mathbb{Q}_{\infty},E[p^{\infty}])^{\vee}\right)=0&\textrm{ when }n\textrm{ is odd},\\
\mu\left(\Lambda_{\mathrm{Iw}}/L^{+}_{p}(E)\right)=\mu\left(\mathrm{Sel}^{-}(\mathbb{Q}_{\infty},E[p^{\infty}])^{\vee}\right)=0&\textrm{ when }n\textrm{ is even}.\end{array}

Remark 10.3
.

Theorem
10.2
.(1) generalizes to Hida families
[
EPW06
]

The Néron periods of optimal elliptic curves are optimal periods
[
Pol03
, Rem. 5.4 and 5.5]
and
[
Ste85
, Thm. 2.1]
.

We assume Conjecture
1.10
in this section again.

10.1.
Mazur–Tate elements and the conjecture of Coates–Sujatha

Recall the decomposition of finite layer Coleman maps

\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(k-r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Col}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}}),r}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\Lambda_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{i}\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{n-1,p},T_{f,i}(k-r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bigoplus_{i}\mathrm{Col}^{(i)}_{\mathbb{Q}_{n-1},r}}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{i}\Lambda^{(i)}_{n}.}

Then Theorem
1.9
shows that

(10.1)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathrm{Col}^{(i)}_{\mathbb{Q}_{n-1},r}\left(\mathrm{loc}_{p}\left(\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}_{n-1},\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,i,k-r)\right)\right)\\
&=\omega^{i}\left(\mathrm{Col}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}}),r}\left(\mathrm{loc}_{p}\left(\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}}),\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,k-r)\right)\right)\right)\\
&=\omega^{i}\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}}),r}(\overline{f})\right)\in\Lambda^{(i)}_{n}.\end{split}

Since

\omega^{i}\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}}),r}(\overline{f})\right)=\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{n-1}\mathbb{Z}}\left(\sum_{a\in(\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\lambda^{\pm,\mathrm{opt}}(\overline{f},z^{r-1},a(1+bp),p^{n})\cdot\omega^{i}(a)\right)\cdot\sigma_{1+bp},

if
\sum_{a\in(\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\overline{\lambda^{\pm,\mathrm{opt}}(\overline{f},z^{r-1},a(1+bp),p^{n})}\cdot\overline{\omega^{i}(a)}\neq 0

for some
n\geq 1
,
then
\omega^{i}\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}}),r}(\overline{f})\right)\in\Lambda^{(i)}_{n}
is non-zero modulo
\pi
.
Furthermore, (
10.1
) implies that

\mathfrak{z}_{\mathbb{Q}_{n-1},\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,i,k-r)\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{n-1,p},T_{f,i}(k-r))

is non-zero mod
\pi
and

\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathbb{Q},\delta_{f,\mathrm{cris}}}(f,i,k-r)\in\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f,i}(k-r))

is also non-zero mod
\pi
.
By the Euler system divisibility (Theorem
4.16
), we also have

\mu\left(\mathrm{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f,i}(k-r))\right)=0
.

10.2.
Elliptic curves with good ordinary reduction

Let
E
be an (optimal) elliptic curve over
\mathbb{Q}
with non-anomalous good ordinary reduction at
p
corresponding to
f
, i.e.
p\nmid a_{p}(f)\cdot(a_{p}(f)-1)
.
Let
n\geq 0
be an integer.
Write

\theta_{\mathbb{Q}_{n}}(E)=\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n+1}})/\mathbb{Q}_{n}}\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n+1}}),1}(f)\right)
,

\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n+1}})}(E)=\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{n+1}}\cdot\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n+1}}),1}(f)-\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\cdot\nu_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}})/\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n+1}})}\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}}),1}(f)\right)\right),

and

\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}_{n}}(E)=\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n+1}})/\mathbb{Q}_{n}}\left(\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n+1}})}(E)\right)
.
Then
\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}_{n}}(E)
forms a compatible sequence in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{n}/\mathbb{Q})]
and the inverse limit is the
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{p}(E)
of
E
in
\Lambda^{(0)}_{\mathrm{Iw}}
.

Proposition 10.4
.

If
p\nmid a_{p}(f)\cdot(a_{p}(f)-1)
, then

\theta_{\mathbb{Q}_{n}}(E)
is a multiple of
\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}_{n}}(E)
by an element of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{n}/\mathbb{Q})]
, i.e.

\theta_{\mathbb{Q}_{n}}(E)=c\cdot\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}_{n}}(E)

where
c\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{n}/\mathbb{Q})]
.

Proof.

We use induction on
n
.
We write
\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}}(E)=\dfrac{L(f_{\alpha},1)}{\Omega^{+}_{E}}
.
By the interpolation formula, we have

\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}}(E)=\left(1-\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\right)\cdot\theta_{\mathbb{Q}}(E)

and
\left(1-\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\right)
is invertible in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
since
p\nmid(a_{p}(f)-1)
.
Looking at the norm relation in
[
MT87
,
\lx@sectionsign
1.3]
and the definition of
\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p})}(E)
, we also have

\pi_{1,0}(\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p})}(E))=\left(1-\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\right)\cdot\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}}(E)
.
We compute

\displaystyle\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p})}(E)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha}\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p})}(E)-\frac{1}{\alpha}\cdot\nu_{0,1}(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}}(E))\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha}\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p})}(E)-\frac{1}{\alpha}\cdot\nu_{0,1}\left(\left(1-\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\right)^{-1}\cdot\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}}(E)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha}\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p})}(E)-\frac{1}{\alpha}\cdot\nu_{0,1}\left(\left(1-\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\right)^{-2}\cdot\pi_{1,0}(\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p})}(E))\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha}\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p})}(E)-\frac{1}{\alpha}\cdot a\cdot\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p})}(E)\right)

where
a
in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p})/\mathbb{Q})]
.
Thus, we have

\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p})}(E)=\left(\alpha+\dfrac{1}{\alpha}\cdot a\right)\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p})}(E)
.

Now suppose that

\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n-1}})}(E)=b\cdot\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n-1}})}(E)

with
b\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n-1}})/\mathbb{Q})]
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}})}(E)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha^{n}}\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}})}(E)-\frac{1}{\alpha}\cdot\nu_{n,n-1}(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n-1}})}(E))\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha^{n}}\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}})}(f)-\frac{1}{\alpha}\cdot\nu_{n,n-1}(b\cdot\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n-1}})}(E))\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha^{n}}\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}})}(f)-\frac{1}{\alpha}\cdot\nu_{n,n-1}(b\cdot\pi_{n,n-1}(\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}})}(E)))\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha^{n}}\left(\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}})}(f)-\frac{1}{\alpha}\cdot c\cdot\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}})}(E))\right)

where
c\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}})/\mathbb{Q})]
.
In the same manner, we have

\theta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}})}(f)=\left(\alpha^{n}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\cdot c\right)\cdot\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}})}(E).

By using the decomposition via the Teichmüller character, the conclusion follows.
∎

Corollary 10.5
.

Let
\mathfrak{a}
be an ideal of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{n}/\mathbb{Q})]
.
If
\vartheta_{\mathbb{Q}_{n}}(E)\in\mathfrak{a}
, then
\theta_{\mathbb{Q}_{n}}(E)\in\mathfrak{a}
.

If we take
\mathfrak{a}=p\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{n}/\mathbb{Q})]
, then we immediately obtain

\mu\left(\Lambda_{\mathrm{Iw}}/L_{p}(E)\right)=0
from the above corollary.
Also, Corollary
10.2
.(1) follows from the Euler system divisibility of the main conjecture of Mazur–Greenberg, which is equivalent to Theorem
4.16
.

10.3.
Elliptic curves with supersingular reduction

We assume that
f
corresponds to an (optimal) elliptic curve over
\mathbb{Q}
with good supersingular reduction at
p\geq 3
.
We further assume that

a_{p}(f)=0
,
and indeed it always holds if
p\geq 5
due to the Hasse bound.

We recall the identification

\Lambda^{(0)}_{\mathrm{Iw}}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{\infty}/\mathbb{Q})\rrbracket
with
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket X\rrbracket
defined by sending a topological generator of
\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{\infty}/\mathbb{Q})
to
1+X
.
Let
\omega^{\pm}_{0}(X):=X
,
\widetilde{\omega}^{\pm}_{0}(X):=1
, and

\textstyle{{\displaystyle\omega^{+}_{n}=\omega^{+}_{n}(X):=X\cdot\prod_{2\leq m\leq n,m:\textrm{ even}}\Phi_{m}(1+X)},}
\textstyle{{\displaystyle\omega^{-}_{n}=\omega^{-}_{n}(X):=X\cdot\prod_{1\leq m\leq n,m:\textrm{ odd}}\Phi_{m}(1+X)},}
\textstyle{{\displaystyle\widetilde{\omega}^{+}_{n}=\widetilde{\omega}^{+}_{n}(X):=\prod_{2\leq m\leq n,m:\textrm{ even}}\Phi_{m}(1+X)},}
\textstyle{{\displaystyle\widetilde{\omega}^{-}_{n}=\widetilde{\omega}^{-}_{n}(X):=\prod_{1\leq m\leq n,m:\textrm{ odd}}\Phi_{m}(1+X)}.}

Then we have
\omega_{n}(X)=\omega^{\pm}_{n}(X)\cdot\widetilde{\omega}^{\mp}_{n}(X)
, respectively. We also regard

\omega^{\pm}_{n}
,
\widetilde{\omega}^{\pm}_{n}
as elements in
\Lambda^{(0)}_{n+1}
or
\Lambda^{(0)}_{\mathrm{Iw}}
via the identification.

Proposition 10.6

(Pollack)
.

\theta_{n}(E)\equiv\widetilde{\omega}^{\mp}_{n}\cdot L^{\mp}_{p}(E)\ (\text{mod}\ \omega_{n})

in
\Lambda^{(0)}_{n+1}
if
n
is even/odd, respectively.

Proof.

See
[
Pol03
, Prop. 6.18]
.
∎

If the numerical criterion in Theorem
10.1
holds,
then
\theta_{n-1}(E)
does not vanish modulo
p
.
By Proposition
10.6
, if
n-1
is even (resp. odd), then
L^{-}_{p}(E)
(resp.
L^{+}_{p}(E)
) does not vanish modulo
p
; thus,
\mu\left(\mathrm{Sel}^{+}(\mathbb{Q}_{\infty},E[p^{\infty}])^{\vee}\right)=0
(resp.
\mu\left(\mathrm{Sel}^{-}(\mathbb{Q}_{\infty},E[p^{\infty}])^{\vee}\right)=0
) by the Euler system divisibility of the
\pm
-main conjectures, which is equivalent to Theorem
4.16
.
Thus, Corollary
10.2
.(2) follows.

11.
Applications to Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer conjecture

We discuss two applications to Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer conjecture. More general applications are studied in the subsequent works
[
Kimb
,
Kima
]
.

11.1.
On the
p
-part of the BSD formula for elliptic curves of analytic rank one

We first discuss an application of Theorem
1.5
and Corollary
1.6
to the arithmetic of elliptic curves of analytic rank one via the work of Kobayashi on
p
-adic Gross–Zagier formula
[
Kob13
]
.

Let
E
be an (optimal) elliptic curve of conductor
N
with good reduction at
p>2
and
\mathcal{K}
an imaginary quadratic field such that all the prime factors of
Np
split in
\mathcal{K}
and
L(E_{\mathcal{K}},1)\neq 0

where
E_{\mathcal{K}}
is the quadratic twist of
E
by
\mathcal{K}
. See
[
BFH90
,
MM91
]
for the existence of such an imaginary quadratic field. Let
f
be the newform corresponding to
E
and
f_{\mathrm{min}}
the newform of minimal level congruent to
f
modulo
p
obtained by level lowering
[
DT94
]
. The same notational rule applies to
E_{\mathcal{K}}
.

Theorem 11.1

(Kobayashi)
.

Suppose that

(1)

\mathrm{ord}_{s=1}L(E,s)=1
,

(2)

E[p]
is a surjective Galois representation, and

(3)

the
p
-adic height pairing is non-degenerate and
a_{p}(E)\not\equiv 1\pmod{p}
when
p
is good ordinary for
E
.

If

(i)

\widetilde{\delta}_{m}(f_{\mathrm{min}},1)\neq 0,\widetilde{\delta}_{m_{\mathcal{K}}}(f_{\mathcal{K},\mathrm{min}},1)\neq 0
in
\mathbb{F}_{p}

for some square-free products
m
and
m_{\mathcal{K}}
of Kolyvagin primes for
f_{\mathrm{min}}
and
f_{\mathcal{K},\mathrm{min}}
, respectively, and

(ii)

{\displaystyle\sum_{a\in(\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\overline{\lambda^{\pm,\mathrm{opt}}(f,1,a(1+bp),p^{n})}\neq 0,\displaystyle\sum_{a\in(\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\overline{\lambda^{\pm,\mathrm{opt}}(f_{\mathcal{K}},1,a(1+b^{\prime}p),p^{n})}\neq 0}

in
\mathbb{F}_{p}

for some
b,b^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{n-1}\mathbb{Z}
with
n\geq 1
,

then the
p
-part of BSD formula for
E
holds, i.e.

\left|\dfrac{L^{\prime}(E,1)}{\Omega_{E}\cdot\mathrm{Reg}(E/\mathbb{Q})}\right|_{p}=\left|\#\textrm{{SH}}(E/\mathbb{Q})\cdot\prod_{\ell|N}c_{\ell}(E/\mathbb{Q})\right|_{p}

where

\Omega_{E}
is the real period of
E
,

\mathrm{Reg}(E/\mathbb{Q}):=\dfrac{\langle y,y\rangle_{\mathrm{NT}}}{[E(\mathbb{Q}):\mathbb{Z}\cdot y]^{2}}
for any non-torsion
y\in E(\mathbb{Q})
,

\langle-,-\rangle_{\mathrm{NT}}
is the Néron–Tate height pairing,
and
c_{\ell}(E/\mathbb{Q})
is the local Tamagawa factor of
E
at
\ell
.
If all the local Tamagawa factors of
E
are prime to
p
, then we can bypass the numerical computation (ii).

Proof.

This is the combination of Theorem
1.5
, Corollary
1.6
, and
[
Kob13
, Corollary 1.3.(3)]
.
∎

See
[
BPS
]
for the recent developments on the non-degeneracy of the
p
-adic height pairing for the ordinary case.
One advantage of Theorem
11.1
is that no assumption on the conductor is imposed.

11.2.
Kato’s Kolyvagin systems and Heegner point Kolyvagin systems

We study the applications of Theorem
1.5
to Kolyvagin’s conjecture on the indivisibility of Heegner point Kolyvagin systems following Wei Zhang’s work
[
Zha14
]
.
The immediate consequences include
the
p
-converse to a theorem of Gross–Zagier and Kolyvagin,
the structure theorem on the
p
-part of Tate–Shafarevich groups, and
the
p
-part of the BSD formula of modular abelian varieties of analytic rank one.
Since Wei Zhang’s work already completes the
p
-ordinary case, our main focus lies in the
p
-non-ordinary case.

11.2.1.
The anticyclotomic setting

Let
p>3
be a prime and
f\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
a newform with
(N,p)=1
.
Fix a prime
\pi
of the Hecke field
F
of
f
lying above
p
.
Let
\mathcal{K}
be an imaginary quadratic field of discriminant
D_{\mathcal{K}}<0
with
(D_{\mathcal{K}},Np)=1
.
Write
N=N^{+}\cdot N^{-}
where a prime factor of
N^{+}
splits in
\mathcal{K}
and a prime factor of
N^{-}
is inert in
\mathcal{K}
.
We always assume that
N^{-}
is square-free and denote by
\nu(N^{-})
the number of prime factors of
N^{-}
.
Then the root number for
f
over
\mathcal{K}
is
(-1)^{1+\nu(N^{-})}
. In this subsection, we assume that
\nu(N^{-})
is even, i.e. the
generalized Heegner hypothesis
for
(f,\mathcal{K})
is satisfied.

A prime
\ell
is an
anticyclotomic Kolyvagin prime for
(f,\mathcal{K},\pi)
if
\ell
is prime to
N\cdot D_{\mathcal{K}}\cdot p
, inert in
\mathcal{K}
, and the Kolyvagin index
M(\ell):=\mathrm{min}(v_{p}(\ell+1),v_{\pi}(a_{\ell}(f)))
is strictly positive. The last condition is equivalent to that
\ell\equiv-1\pmod{p}
and
a_{\ell}(f)\equiv 0\pmod{\pi}
.
Denote by
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{ac}}
the set of square-free products of anticyclotomic Kolyvagin primes for
(f,\mathcal{K},\pi)
.
Define
M(n)=\mathrm{min}\{M(\ell):\ell|n\}
if
n>1
and
M(1)=\infty
where
n\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{ac}}
.

Denote by
\mathcal{K}(n)
the ring class field of conductor
n
of
\mathcal{K}
and by
A_{f}
the modular abelian variety associated to
f
.
To each Heegner point
y(n)\in A_{f}(\mathcal{K}(n))
of conductor
n
and
M\leq M(n)
, there exists a cohomology class

c^{\mathrm{Heeg}}_{M}(n)\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathcal{K},T_{f}/\pi^{M}T_{f})

where
n
runs over the square-free products of anticyclotomic Kolyvagin primes. See
[
Zha14
,
\lx@sectionsign
3.7]
for the explicit construction of
c^{\mathrm{Heeg}}_{M}(n)
.

Following the terminology of
[
MR04
]
, suitably modified for the Heegner point
setting
[
How04a
,
How04b
]
, we call the collection

\boldsymbol{\kappa}^{\mathrm{Heeg}}=\left\{c^{\mathrm{Heeg}}_{M}(n)\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathcal{K},T_{f}/\pi^{M}T_{f}):n\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{ac}},M\leq M(n)\right\}

the Heegner point Kolyvagin system
11
11

11

We do not distinguish Kolyvagin systems and weak Kolyvagin systems seriously in this subsection.
for
(f,\mathcal{K},\pi)
.

11.2.2.
Kolyvagin conjecture

For a fixed
n\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{ac}}
,
define the divisibility index

\mathscr{M}(n)

by the maximal non-negative integer (or the infinity)
\mathscr{M}
such that

c^{\mathrm{Heeg}}_{M}(n)\in\pi^{\mathscr{M}}\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathcal{K},T_{f}/\pi^{M}T_{f})

for all
M\leq M(n)
. Define

\mathscr{M}_{r}:=\mathrm{min}_{\nu(n)=r}\mathscr{M}(n)

and Kolyvagin shows that
\mathscr{M}_{r}\geq\mathscr{M}_{r+1}\geq 0
.
We also define
\mathscr{M}_{\infty}(f):=\lim_{r\to\infty}\mathscr{M}_{r}
as the minimum of
\mathscr{M}_{r}
for varying
r\geq 0
. The following conjecture is due to Kolyvagin (as stated in
[
Kol91
, Conj. A]
and
[
Zha14
, Conj. 3.2]
).

Conjecture 11.2

(Kolyvagin)
.

If
\rho_{f}
has large image, then
\boldsymbol{\kappa}^{\mathrm{Heeg}}\neq 0
. Equivalently,
\mathscr{M}_{\infty}(f)<\infty
.

11.2.3.
Statement of the main results

For a residual Galois representation
\overline{\rho}
, denote by
S_{2}(\overline{\rho})
the set of newforms of weight two and trivial nebentypus whose residual representations are isomorphic to
\overline{\rho}
and levels are not divisible by
p
.
For a given
f\in S_{2}(\overline{\rho})
, denote by
f_{\mathrm{min}}\in S_{2}(\overline{\rho})
any congruent newform of minimal level via level lowering.
Denote by
\mathrm{Ram}(f,\overline{\rho})
the set of primes
\ell
dividing
N
exactly and
\overline{\rho}
is ramified at
\ell
.

The following statement is a slight refinement of Wei Zhang’s result on Kolyvagin’s conjecture
[
Zha14
, Thm. 1.1 and Thm. 9.3]
.
Let
f=\sum_{n\geq 1}a_{n}(f)q^{n}\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be a newform and
\pi
a fixed prime of the Hecke field
F
of
f
lying above a prime
p\geq 5
with
(N,p)=1
.
Let
\mathcal{K}
be an imaginary quadratic field of discriminant
D_{\mathcal{K}}
with
(Np,D_{\mathcal{K}})=1
.
Denote by
f_{\mathcal{K}}
the twist of
f
by the quadratic character associated to
\mathcal{K}
.

Theorem 11.3

(Kolyvagin’s conjecture)
.

Suppose that the triple
(f,\mathcal{K},\pi)
satisfies the following properties.

(1)

\rho_{f}
has large image.

(2)

N^{-}
is square-free with even number of prime factors.

(3)

\mathrm{Ram}(f,\overline{\rho})
contains all primes
\ell|N^{-}
such that
\ell\equiv\pm 1\pmod{p}
.

(4)

\mathrm{Ram}(f,\overline{\rho})
contains all primes
\ell
such that
\ell\|N^{+}
.

(5)

If
F\neq\mathbb{Q}
, then we have
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},\overline{\rho})=\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},\overline{\rho})=0
for all prime
\ell
with
\ell^{2}|N^{+}
.

If

(i)

\widetilde{\delta}_{m}(f_{\mathrm{min}},1)\neq 0
and
\widetilde{\delta}_{m_{\mathcal{K}}}(f_{\mathcal{K},\mathrm{min}},1)\neq 0

in
\mathbb{F}_{\pi}

for some square-free products
m
and
m_{\mathcal{K}}
of Kolyvagin primes for
f_{\mathrm{min}}
and
f_{\mathcal{K},\mathrm{min}}
, respectively, and

(ii)

{\displaystyle\sum_{a\in(\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\overline{\lambda^{\pm,\mathrm{opt}}(f,1,a(1+bp),p^{n})}\neq 0,\displaystyle\sum_{a\in(\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z})^{\times}}\overline{\lambda^{\pm,\mathrm{opt}}(f_{\mathcal{K}},1,a(1+b^{\prime}p),p^{n})}\neq 0}

in
\mathbb{F}_{\pi}

for some
b,b^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{n-1}\mathbb{Z}
with
n\geq 1
, respectively,

then
\boldsymbol{\kappa}^{\mathrm{Heeg}}
is
\pi
-indivisible, i.e.
c^{\mathrm{Heeg}}_{1}(n)
does not vanish for some
n\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{ac}}
. Equivalently,
\mathscr{M}_{\infty}(f)=0
.
Thus, Kolyvagin conjecture (Conjecture
11.2
) holds for
(f,\mathcal{K},\pi)
, i.e.
\boldsymbol{\kappa}^{\mathrm{Heeg}}
is non-zero. Equivalently,
\mathscr{M}_{\infty}(f)<\infty
.

Proof.

This is an application of Theorem
9.1
and Theorem
10.1
to the main result of
[
Zha14
]
.
The most argument in
[
Zha14
]
immediately applies to this setting.
In
[
Zha14
]
, Hypothesis
\heartsuit
(Assumption
11.15
) is imposed, which includes Condition (3), (4) and (5). The relaxation of Hypothesis
\heartsuit
is explained in §
11.2.4
.
The
p
-ordinary condition is also imposed therein in order to invoke the
p
-part of the BSD formula for rank zero elliptic curves due to Kato and Skinner–Urban
[
Kat04
,
SU14
]
. See
[
Zha14
, Thm. 7.1 and Thm. 7.2]
. The
p
-ordinary condition is replaced by (i) and (ii), and this replacement is explained in §
11.2.5
, especially, Proposition
11.17
.
∎

One interesting feature of Theorem
11.3
is that Kolyvagin conjecture on the
p
-indivisibility of Heegner point Kolyvagin systems (for non-ordinary primes) can also be verified numerically.
Another interesting aspect is that the
p
-indivisibility of Kato’s Kolyvagin systems and the
p
-indivisibility of Heegner point Kolyvagin systems are tightly related although the natures of these Kolyvagin systems are
very different
.
The following corollary is a conceptual version of Theorem
11.3
explicitly revealing this aspect, which is more than the analogy.

Denote by
\boldsymbol{\kappa}^{\mathrm{Kato}}
the Kato’s Kolyvagin system associated to the canonical Kato’s Euler system.

Corollary 11.4

(“derived” Perrin-Riou conjecture)
.

Let
(f,\mathcal{K},\pi)
be as in Theorem
11.3
.
If

(i)

\boldsymbol{\kappa}^{\mathrm{Kato}}
is
\pi
-indivisible for
f_{\mathrm{min}}
and
f_{\mathcal{K},\mathrm{min}}
(“primitive” in the sense of
[
MR04
]
) and

(ii)

the cyclotomic
\mu
-invariants for fine Selmer groups vanish for
f
and
f_{\mathcal{K}}
,

then
\boldsymbol{\kappa}^{\mathrm{Heeg}}
is
\pi
-indivisible for
(f,\mathcal{K},p)
.

Remark 11.5
.

Corollary
11.4
asserts that

the mod
p
non-vanishing of Kato’s Kolyvagin systems implies the mod
p
non-vanishing of Heegner point Kolyvagin systems
(modulo some congruence argument via vanishing of the
\mu
-invariants).

Thus, it can be regarded as a “derived” variant of Perrin-Riou conjecture on the relation between Kato’s zeta elements and Heegner points at the bottom layer
[
PR93
]
.

Remark 11.6
.

In order to prove Kolyvagin conjecture at non-ordinary primes
unconditionally
, we need the equality of the cyclotomic Iwasawa main conjecture for non-ordinary modular forms of weight two and
general
level.
For example, the square-free level assumption excludes
f_{\mathcal{K}}
since the level of
f_{\mathcal{K}}
is
N\cdot|D_{\mathcal{K}}|^{2}
. (cf.
[
Wan
,
Spr
,
CÇSS
]
.)

The vanishing order
\mathrm{ord}(\boldsymbol{\kappa}^{\mathrm{Heeg}})
of the Kolyvagin system
\boldsymbol{\kappa}^{\mathrm{Heeg}}
is defined by the minimal number of prime factors of
n\in\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{ac}}
such that

c^{\mathrm{Heeg}}_{M}(n)\neq 0
for some
M\leq M(n)
.
Let
\mathrm{Sel}(\mathcal{K},W_{f}(1))^{\pm}
denote the eigenspace with eigenvalue
\pm 1
of
\mathrm{Sel}(\mathcal{K},W_{f}(1))
under
the complex conjugation
12
12

12

The reader should not confuse this with Kobayashi’s signed Selmer groups.
.
Let
r^{\pm}_{\pi}(f/\mathcal{K})
be the
\mathcal{O}_{\pi}
-corank of
\mathrm{Sel}(\mathcal{K},W_{f}(1))^{\pm}
.
Combining Theorem
11.3
with Kolyvagin’s theorem
[
Kol91
,
Mas19
]
, the following result on the relation between Selmer coranks and the vanishing order of Kolyvagin systems easily follows.

Theorem 11.7

(Selmer coranks and
\mathrm{ord}(\boldsymbol{\kappa}^{\mathrm{Heeg}})
)
.

Let
(f,\mathcal{K},\pi)
be as in Theorem
11.3
.
If the mod
p
numerical criteria (i) and (ii) in Theorem
11.3
hold for
f
and
f_{\mathcal{K}}
, then we have

(1)

\mathrm{ord}(\boldsymbol{\kappa}^{\mathrm{Heeg}})=\mathrm{max}\{r^{+}_{\pi}(f/\mathcal{K}),r^{-}_{\pi}(f/\mathcal{K})\}-1
,

(2)

r^{\bullet}_{\pi}(f/\mathcal{K})=\mathrm{ord}(\boldsymbol{\kappa}^{\mathrm{Heeg}})+1
, and

(3)

0\leq\mathrm{ord}(\boldsymbol{\kappa}^{\mathrm{Heeg}})-r^{-\bullet}_{\pi}(f/\mathcal{K})\equiv 0\pmod{2}
,

where
\bullet
is the sign of
\epsilon(f/\mathbb{Q})\cdot(-1)^{\mathrm{ord}(\boldsymbol{\kappa}^{\mathrm{Heeg}})+1}
and
\epsilon(f/\mathbb{Q})
is the root number of
f
over
\mathbb{Q}
.

Proof.

It essentially follows from Theorem
11.3
and
[
Kol91
, Thm. 4]
.
Indeed, this is
[
Zha14
, Thm. 1.2 and Thm. 11.2]
where the statements are given only for elliptic curves over
\mathbb{Q}
since so is the setting of
[
Kol91
]
. The generalization of the setting to (even higher weight) modular forms is now developed in
[
Mas19
, Thm. 8.4]
.
∎

Theorem 11.8

(
p
-converse to Gross–Zagier and Kolyvagin over
\mathcal{K}
)
.

Let
(f,\mathcal{K},\pi)
be as in Theorem
11.3
, and
assume that the mod
p
numerical criteria (i) and (ii) in Theorem
11.3
hold for
f
and
f_{\mathcal{K}}

If
\mathrm{cork}_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}\mathrm{Sel}(\mathcal{K},W_{f}(1))=1
, then the Heegner point
y_{\mathcal{K}}\in A_{f}(\mathcal{K})
is non-torsion. In particular, we have

\mathrm{ord}_{s=1}(L(f/\mathcal{K},s))=1
,
\mathrm{dim}_{\mathbb{Q}}(A_{f}(\mathcal{K})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})=[F:\mathbb{Q}]
, and
\textrm{{SH}}(A_{f}/\mathcal{K})
is finite.

Proof.

See
[
Zha14
, Proof of Thm. 1.3]
. The “In particular” part follows from Gross–Zagier formula
[
YZZ13
]
and Euler systems of Heegner points
[
Nek07
]
.
∎

Remark 11.9
.

In Theorem
11.8
,
p
does not need to split in
\mathcal{K}/\mathbb{Q}
even in the non-ordinary case.

The following structure theorem of
\textrm{{SH}}(A_{f}/\mathcal{K})
also follows from Theorem
11.3
.

Theorem 11.10

(Structure of
SH
)
.

Let
(f,\mathcal{K},\pi)
be as in Theorem
11.3
, and assume that the mod
p
numerical criteria (i) and (ii) in Theorem
11.3
hold for
f
and
f_{\mathcal{K}}
.
If
\mathrm{cork}_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}\mathrm{Sel}(\mathcal{K},W_{f}(1))=1
, then
we have

\textrm{{SH}}(A_{f}/\mathcal{K})^{\pm}[\pi^{\infty}]\simeq\bigoplus_{i\geq 1}\mathrm{End}_{\mathbb{Z}}(A_{f})/\pi^{a^{\pm}_{i}}\mathrm{End}_{\mathbb{Z}}(A_{f})

with
a^{\pm}_{1}\geq a^{\pm}_{2}\geq\cdots
determined by the following formula

\displaystyle a^{\mathrm{sgn}\left(\epsilon(f/\mathbb{Q})\cdot(-1)^{\mathrm{ord}(\boldsymbol{\kappa}^{\mathrm{Heeg}})+1}\right)}_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{M}_{\mathrm{ord}(\boldsymbol{\kappa}^{\mathrm{Heeg}})+2i-1}-\mathscr{M}_{\mathrm{ord}(\boldsymbol{\kappa}^{\mathrm{Heeg}})+2i},

\displaystyle a^{\mathrm{sgn}\left(-\epsilon(f/\mathbb{Q})\cdot(-1)^{\mathrm{ord}(\boldsymbol{\kappa}^{\mathrm{Heeg}})+1}\right)}_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{M}_{\mathrm{ord}(\boldsymbol{\kappa}^{\mathrm{Heeg}})+2i-2}-\mathscr{M}_{\mathrm{ord}(\boldsymbol{\kappa}^{\mathrm{Heeg}})+2i-1}.

Proof.

See
[
Zha14
, Rem. 18]
,
[
Kol91
, Thm. 1]
, and
[
Mas19
, Thm. 8.4]
.
∎

In order to deduce the corresponding result for
f
over
\mathbb{Q}
from Theorem
11.8
, we need to make the following choice of auxiliary imaginary quadratic fields.

Choice 11.11
.

For a pair
(f,\pi)
, we choose two imaginary quadratic fields
\mathcal{K}_{0}
and
\mathcal{K}_{1}
satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

Both
(f,\mathcal{K}_{0},\pi)
and
(f,\mathcal{K}_{1},\pi)
satisfy all the conditions of Theorem
11.3
.

(2)

L(f_{\mathcal{K}_{0}},1)\neq 0
.

(3)

L^{\prime}(f_{\mathcal{K}_{1}},1)\neq 0
when
\epsilon(f/\mathbb{Q})=1
.

See
[
Zha14
, Proof of Thm. 1.4]
for making such choices of
\mathcal{K}_{0}
and
\mathcal{K}_{1}
.

Theorem 11.12

(
p
-converse to Gross–Zagier and Kolyvagin over
\mathbb{Q}
)
.

Let
p
be a prime
\geq 5
and
f\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
a newform.
Let
\mathcal{K}_{0}
and
\mathcal{K}_{1}
be the imaginary quadratic fields chosen in Choice
11.11
.
Assume that
(f,\pi)
satisfies the following statements.

(1)

\rho_{f}
has large image.

(2)

If
\ell\equiv\pm 1\pmod{p}
and
\ell
divides
N
exactly, then
\overline{\rho}_{f}
is ramified at
\ell
.

(3)

If
F\neq\mathbb{Q}
, then we have
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},\overline{\rho}_{f})=\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},\overline{\rho}_{f})=0
for all prime
\ell
with
\ell^{2}|N^{+}
.

If the mod
p
numerical criteria (i) and (ii) in Theorem
11.3
hold for
f
,
f_{\mathcal{K}_{0}}
, and
f_{\mathcal{K}_{1}}
, then we have the following statements.

(a)

If
\mathrm{cork}_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}\mathrm{Sel}(\mathbb{Q},W_{f}(1))=1
, then

\mathrm{ord}_{s=1}(L(f,s))=1
,
\mathrm{dim}_{\mathbb{Q}}(A_{f}(\mathbb{Q})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})=[F:\mathbb{Q}]
, and
\textrm{{SH}}(A_{f}/\mathbb{Q})
is finite.

(b)

If
\mathrm{ord}_{s=1}L(f,s)>1
,
then
\mathrm{cork}_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}\mathrm{Sel}(\mathbb{Q},W_{f}(1))
is at least two (three, resp.) if
\epsilon(E/\mathbb{Q})
is 1 (
-1
, resp.).

Proof.

See
[
Zha14
, Proof of Thm. 1.4]
. Note that (a) uses
\mathcal{K}_{0}
and (b) uses
\mathcal{K}_{1}
.
∎

Theorem 11.13

(Rank one BSD formula for
A_{f}
over
\mathcal{K}
)
.

Let
(f,\mathcal{K},\pi)
be as in Theorem
11.3
and
assume that the mod
p
numerical criteria (i) and (ii) in Theorem
11.3
hold for
f
and
f_{\mathcal{K}}
.
If
\mathrm{ord}_{s=1}L(f/\mathcal{K},s)=1
, then the
\pi
-part of the BSD formula for
A_{f}
over
\mathcal{K}
holds, i.e.

\mathrm{ord}_{\pi}\left(\dfrac{L^{*}(A_{f}/\mathcal{K},1)}{\Omega^{\mathrm{can}}_{A_{f}}\cdot|D_{\mathcal{K}}|^{-1/2}\cdot\mathrm{Reg}(A_{f}/\mathcal{K})}\right)=\mathrm{length}_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}\textrm{{SH}}(A_{f}/\mathcal{K})[\pi^{\infty}]+\mathrm{ord}_{\pi}\left(\prod_{\ell|N^{-}}c_{\ell}(A_{f}/\mathcal{K})\right)

where

L^{*}(A_{f}/\mathcal{K},1)
is the leading term of the Taylor expansion of
L(A_{f}/\mathcal{K},s)
at
s=1
,

\Omega^{\mathrm{can}}_{A_{f}}
is the canonical period of
A_{f}
,

\mathrm{Reg}(A_{f}/\mathcal{K}):=\dfrac{\langle y,y\rangle_{\mathrm{NT}}}{[A_{f}(\mathcal{K}):\mathrm{End}_{\mathbb{Z}}(A_{f})\cdot y]^{2}}
for any non-torsion
y\in A_{f}(\mathcal{K})
,

\langle-,-\rangle_{\mathrm{NT}}
is the Néron–Tate height pairing,
and

c_{\ell}(A_{f}/\mathbb{Q})
and
c_{\ell}(A_{f}/\mathcal{K})
are local Tamagawa factors.

Proof.

See
[
Zha14
, Proof of Thm. 10.2]
. The Tamagawa factors at primes dividing
N^{+}
are
\pi
-adic units due to Assumption (4) in Theorem
11.3
.
∎

Theorem 11.14

(Rank one BSD formula for
A_{f}
over
\mathbb{Q}
)
.

Let
(f,\pi)
be as in Theorem
11.12
and assume that the mod
p
numerical criteria (i) and (ii) in Theorem
11.3
hold for
f
and
f_{\mathcal{K}_{0}}
(as in Choice
11.11
).
If
\mathrm{ord}_{s=1}L(f,s)=1
, then the
\pi
-part of the BSD formula for
A_{f}
over
\mathbb{Q}
holds, i.e.

\mathrm{ord}_{\pi}\left(\dfrac{L^{*}(A_{f},1)}{\Omega_{A_{f}}\cdot\mathrm{Reg}(A_{f}/\mathbb{Q})}\right)=\mathrm{length}_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}\textrm{{SH}}(A_{f}/\mathbb{Q})[\pi^{\infty}]+\mathrm{ord}_{\pi}\left(\prod_{\ell|N}c_{\ell}(A_{f}/\mathbb{Q})\right)

where

\Omega_{A_{f}}
is the real period of
A_{f}
and

\mathrm{Reg}(A_{f}/\mathbb{Q}):=\dfrac{\langle y,y\rangle_{\mathrm{NT}}}{[A_{f}(\mathbb{Q}):\mathrm{End}_{\mathbb{Z}}(A_{f})\cdot y]^{2}}
for any non-torsion
y\in A_{f}(\mathbb{Q})
.

Proof.

See
[
Zha14
, Proof of Thm. 10.3]
.
∎

11.2.4.
Relaxing Hypothesis
\heartsuit

We recall “Hypothesis
\heartsuit
” in
[
Zha14
, (xv), Notations]
.

Assumption 11.15

(Hypothesis
\heartsuit
)
.

Let
\mathrm{Ram}(f,\overline{\rho})
be the set of primes
\ell
dividing
N
exactly and
\overline{\rho}
is ramified at
\ell
.

(1)

\mathrm{Ram}(f,\overline{\rho})
contains all primes
\ell|N^{-}
such that
\ell\equiv\pm 1\pmod{p}
.

(2)

\mathrm{Ram}(f,\overline{\rho})
contains all primes
\ell
such that
\ell\|N^{+}

(3)

If
N
is not square-free, then
\#\mathrm{Ram}(f,\overline{\rho})\geq 1
.

(4)

If
N
is not square-free, then
\mathrm{Ram}(f,\overline{\rho})
contains a prime
\ell\mid N^{-}
or there exist at least two prime factors
\ell\|N^{+}
.

(5)

If
F\neq\mathbb{Q}
, then we have
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},\overline{\rho})=\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},\overline{\rho})=0
for all prime
\ell
with
\ell^{2}|N^{+}
.

The following remark explains how each condition in Hypothesis
\heartsuit
is used in Wei Zhang’s argument and shows (3) and (4) are unnecessary by improving a certain argument therein.

Remark 11.16
.

(1)

Assumption
11.15
.(1) is important to have the mod
p
multiplicity one of the character group of Shimura curves of discriminant divisible by
N^{-}
in
[
Zha14
, Lem. 3.3 and Thm. 4.3]
.
This is related to “Mazur’s principle”. See also
[
PW11b
]
and
[
Zha14
, (4.8)]
.

(2)

Assumption
11.15
.(2) implies that all the Tamagawa factors at primes dividing
N^{+}
are
\pi
-adic units.
The
\pi
-indivisibility of Heegner point Kolyvagin systems fails without this assumption. See
[
Jet08
]
.

(3)

Assumptions
11.15
.(3) and (4) are used only in
[
Zha14
, Thm. 6.4]
on the congruence number-Tamagawa exponent computation based on the approach of Ribet–Takahashi
[
RT97
,
Tak01
]
.
The approach of Ribet–Takahashi becomes subtle when the level is not square-free. See
[
ARS12
]
for the comparison between modular degrees and congruence numbers.
Indeed,
[
Zha14
, Thm. 6.4]
can be replaced by the work of the author with K. Ota
[
KO23
]
based on an
R=\mathbb{T}
theorem without these assumptions.

(4)

Assumptions
11.15
.(5) is used only in
[
Zha14
, Thm. 5.2]
.
It is automatic in the case of elliptic curves as proved in
[
Zha14
, Lem. 5.1.(2)]
.

11.2.5.
Replacing the
p
-ordinary condition

As already pointed out in
[
Zha14
,
\lx@sectionsign
7.1]
, the
p
-ordinary assumption is only used in
[
Zha14
]
in order to invoke the
p
-part of the BSD formula for the level raising newforms of
f
and
f_{\mathcal{K}}
of level
Nq
and
N\cdot|D_{\mathcal{K}}|^{2}\cdot q
at a 1-admissible prime
q
(cf.
[
Zha14
, (xiv), Notations]
) whose Selmer coranks are zero. See
[
Zha14
, Thm. 7.1 and Proof of Thm. 7.2]
how the work of Kato
[
Kat04
]
and Skinner–Urban
[
SU14
]
are used.
This is crucial for the first step in the induction argument of the proof of Kolyvagin conjecture
[
Zha14
, Thm. 9.1]
.

If we can verify the
p
-part of the rank zero BSD formula for the non-ordinary case, then all the results in
[
Zha14
]
immediately generalizes to the non-ordinary case.

Proposition 11.17
.

Suppose that
\overline{\rho}_{f}
is irreducible and Kato’s main conjecture holds for
T_{f}(1)
at a good prime
p>2
and
\mathrm{Sel}(\mathbb{Q},W_{f}(1))
is finite.
Then the
\pi
-part of the Bloch–Kato conjecture holds for
f
, i.e.

\mathrm{ord}_{\pi}\left(\dfrac{L(f,1)}{\Omega^{+}_{f}}\right)=\mathrm{length}_{\mathcal{O}_{\pi}}\left(\mathrm{Sel}(\mathbb{Q},W_{f}(1))\right)+\mathrm{ord}_{\pi}\left(\prod_{\ell|N}c_{\ell}(A_{f}/\mathbb{Q})\right).

Proof.

We split the proof into two cases.

(1)

When
f
is ordinary at
p
, it follows from the main conjecture of Mazur–Greenberg

[
Kat04
,
\lx@sectionsign
17.13]
and the Euler characteristic of Selmer groups
[
Gre99
, Thm. 4.1]
.

(2)

When
f
is non-ordinary at
p
, it also follows from the signed Iwasawa main conjecture
[
LLZ10
, Cor. 6.6]
, the interpolation formula of signed
p
-adic
L
-function at the trivial character
[
LLZ10
, Prop. 3.28]
, and the Euler characteristic of signed Selmer groups
[
Pon20
, Cor. 2.10]
. See also
[
CÇSS
]
.

∎
